# Sun stop notes

On branch `agent/sun-hook` this file is the copy from main. The hook surge is noted in `HOOK-NOTES.md` next to it;
the polish notebook (mail 34 list) lives on branch `agent/sun`.

Lab notebook of the agent that builds the `sun` stop. Branch `agent/sun`, looks on `agent/sun-looks`.

## Standing facts

- Segments in order: `sun.terrain` (frames 0 to 179), `sun.rise`, `sun.hold`, `sun.travel`. No dive.
- Judged frames: 0 (frame 0), 60 (hook), 90 (terrain), 247 (rise), 360 (hold), 495 (travel).
- Approved images: `references/approved/sun-hero.png`, `references/approved/sun-close.jpg`, both 2816 x 1584.
- VM: 4 cores, 7 GB RAM, 17 GB free disk at the start.

## Log

- Start. Cloned, branch `agent/sun`, `npm ci`, build green. Stand-in compare frames rendered at draft for hold and terrain and pushed as the first looks.
- 03:05 UTC. Reading done (engine, Mars set and plan, relief, cover, sky, measures, recipes). Merged origin/main (2cbceef). Media skill loaded; image tools are `generate_image` (seedream_5 is the model of the approved images), called through the exec tool.

## Builder notes (03:10 UTC)

- `public/assets/sun/web/` at most 35 MB on disk (not 40). Full stays 150 MB. Film is rendered from full.
- Engine requests are expensive for every stop (the hash moves). Keep everything stop-local when it can be. Decision: no engine request for the sky. The hold sky is drawn by my own set as a far card (vertex shader pins it at the far plane, normal blend over the engine sky, alpha left untouched), so its weight is mine and nothing pops at frames 270 and 450.
- The set must also behave in an arriving travel (other stop's travel into the Sun's hold, FarPoint pattern from Mars) and in its own leaving travel. Judged in Phase 5; keep free of pops.
- Frame 0 is the poster frame, first two seconds judged at phone size (R6): finished picture at frame 0, plasma visibly flowing from the first frame.

## Engine facts that shape the design

- Post cannot be changed per stop. Bloom is mix(hdr, bloom, 0.08), streak from values above 14. So glare has to come from real HDR values in my layers.
- Approved pixels used as textures go through `inverseFilmic`, divided by `shot.post.exposure`, with the lens vignette undone.
- Terrain view is its own stage: origin at the spot, camera pose from `plan.terrain(tRel)` = (side u, rise u, -forward u), u from -0.5 to 0.5, looking down -Z at u = 0. Units are mine: camera height 1.
- Rise: terrain view with exit offsets until the cover centre (frame 216), then orbit view from `spot + normal * dive.altitude` to `hold(0)`. The Sun has no dive, so `dive.altitude` only sets where the orbit part of the rise starts. A high start (0.3 to 0.5) keeps G13 easy.
- Sky patch weight is fixed by the engine: 1 in hold and terrain, 0 in the orbit part of a rise and for the leaving stop in travel. For the Sun (rise goes into hold, then travel) that pops at frames 270 and 450. Needs an engine request (an optional weight on the patch, off by default). Not yet written.
- G13 counted frames: 0 to 207, 224 to 449.

## Geometry read from the approved images

- Hero: disc centre (1410.4, 791.8) px, radius 662.3 px, vfov 30, camera z 4.574. Frame reaches 1.196 R up and down, 2.13 R left and right.
- Close: horizon top at about y 0.47 of the height, drops to 0.55 at the left edge and 0.60 at the right. With vfov 55 that is a ground sphere of radius about 32 camera heights, dip about 14 degrees, horizon about 8 camera heights away. Sunspot centre near (0.52, 0.60), arcade feet at (0.375, 0.51) and (0.73, 0.555), apex near y 0.15.

## Design

Orbit view (all stop-local modules in `src/stops/sun/`):
- Globe: emissive sphere. Wrap 8192 x 4096: hero pixels from the 2x copy on the visible side with the limb curve f(mu) divided out, painted far side. Shader multiplies by the measured limb curve at the real view angle. Granulation boil by two-phase flow warp of the wrap along a small flow texture. Active regions pulse through a mask. Patch level(s) around the spot for the rise, copied from the Planet patch logic.
- Corona: one layer on a camera-facing shell, polar texture (angle, radius) from the hero with stars and prominences removed, extended past the hero frame. Angle is measured around the camera to centre axis against a body-fixed up vector, so the hold reproduces the hero and no view has a pinch. Radial two-phase streaming, shimmer, slow angular drift. Covers the disc edge.
- Prominences: card in the hero limb plane (z = 1/4.574 in body space), hero pixels with matte, turning with the globe. Flow along each loop (angle about a loop centre) plus rain along the radius.
- Sky patch: hero sky minus what the film draws itself (corona, prominences, glow), filled behind the disc by one generated image.

Terrain view:
- Ground: image-space grid mesh from the fitted close camera, each vertex at its distance on the ground sphere (radius about 32) plus relief from the picture (cells domed, lanes low, umbra depressed). Texture is the clean plate (close image without loops, min with the approved pixels) at 5632 wide plus side aprons outpainted in the same projection. Foreground apron below the frame as a top-down granulation map (cells at the size of the picture's near cells), blended in over about one cell.
- Loops: additive layer = approved minus clean plate (scene values), on cards hinged on the foot to foot axis with different lean so arcs separate in depth. Flow in bipolar coordinates of the two feet (tau runs foot to foot). Plus true 3D strands (camera-facing ribbons along arcs, streak texture scrolled along them) near the camera so the camera passes under loops in the first two seconds.
- Flow on the ground: cells churn (two-phase warp), penumbra filaments creep toward the umbra centre, spicule fringe flickers on the horizon.
- Sky patch from the close image above the horizon.

Rise: terrain camera lifts and tips toward the surface, a flare grows from the left loop foot (stop-local HDR layer driven by `shot.cover.amount`), plan cover colours gold, then the orbit view starts high above the spot and pulls back to the hold.

## Memory budget, full set (limit 1400 MB, mips count 4/3)

| Asset | Size | MB |
|---|---|---|
| wrap | 8192 x 4096 | 179 |
| spot patch level 1 (and 2 if the predictor asks) | 4096 x 4096 each | 89 each |
| corona polar | 4096 x 1024 | 22 |
| prominence card | 4096 x 4096 | 89 |
| sky hold, no mips | 3520 x 1980 | 28 |
| ground plate with side aprons | about 7040 x 3168 | 119 |
| loops layer | 5632 x 3168 | 95 |
| top-down apron | 4096 x 4096 | 89 |
| sky terrain, no mips | 3520 x 1980 | 28 |
| distance, masks, flow, noise, strands | small | 40 |
| total | | about 870 |

Web set is every file at half size: about 220 MB (limit 450).

## Builder notes on the orbit view (04:00 UTC), to do before READY, worst first

1. Hold motion is too subtle. Crops 330 to 375 look the same. Over the 6 s the granulation must visibly crawl, loops visibly flow along their arcs, corona visibly stream, judged on a 400 px wide strip of 6 frames across 270 to 449 (phone size), not native crops. Zero at holdMid means the phase, not the speed. Exaggerate.
2. Sky card may carry only what is at infinity. Corona rays, loop glow and disc edge residue belong on the Sun's layers. Ghost test at draft, same exposure: frames 180, 247, 269, 270, 449, 450, 495, 540. No fixed ray pattern left in the sky when the disc changes size or place, no doubled stars against the film's stars, no jump 269 to 270 or 449 to 450, film stars must not slide across the card.
3. Fix the dark specks at lower left and the hard dark edge right of the upper right loop at the source.
- Main is about to move (optional `weight(time, view)` on a sky patch, faster sky pass, config keys). Merge when the Builder says so.
- 04:09 UTC. Merged main b4ae65c (engine batch 1, engine hash 434d0a5e367a62fd; older stills are stale). Sky patches may carry `weight(time, view)`. Shader cost rule from the Builder: the software rasterizer computes both sides of an `if` but skips a `for` body, so put costly conditional work in `for (int k = 0; k < (cond ? 1 : 0); k++) { ... }` and read textures inside with textureGrad or textureLod.
- Terrain fit (fit.py): ground sphere radius 20.63 camera heights, limb half angle 72.51 deg, down vector (-0.0205, -0.9361, -0.3512) in the approved camera frame, residual 0.2 deg rms. Plan values: horizonDeg 3.2, dipDeg 17.5. Canvas = approved frame times 1.5 (left -0.25, top -0.205), 8192 x 4608; plate = canvas rows 1728 to 4608.
- plate.py: plate.jpg (ground, plus the horizon glow on black above the horizon), loops.webp (approved minus clean, scene values, crop box 0.22 0.08 0.86 0.66), sky arrays for the terrain sky card. Wide take is histogram matched to the centre.

## Terrain view, first build (04:13 to 04:45 UTC)

- First compare at frame 90 was close to the approved close image. Found and fixed:
  - Lens-shaped orange outlines in the sky (the Builder saw them too): loop mesh vertices above image row 0.14 fell back to a negative ground distance (ray misses the sphere, closest approach behind the camera). `stage.groundDistance` now returns -1 for a miss and the loop mesh uses `look.loops.far` (9) there. Gone.
  - Green and red residue at the loop feet in the plate, red strand traces in the sky: the clean centre was `approved - loops layer`. Now the clean centre is the clean take itself inside a widened loop mask (WIDEN 49 px at 5632) and the approved pixels outside it.
  - Black spikes above the feet: the warm filter of the sky part of the loops layer cut the white core of the fountains. Bright extended light now always passes (blurred luminance difference above 0.12 to 0.3).
  - Green blobs in the ground glow of the loops layer: glow green and blue are capped by the red channel.
  - Sky rectangle seam: the wide take's sky gets its own histogram match (pixels more than 2.5 deg above the limb) and a 380 px feather in the sky (90 px on the ground).
- Ground past the plate (camera higher or further back than the approved pose): no apron asset. The ground point is folded back into the covered ground in ground coordinates before it is projected (`stFold` in `stage.ts`): reflection at the near line (image row of the canvas bottom), at the two side lines (image columns of the canvas sides, as angles about the point where the camera's down axis meets the ground) and at a circle 2.6 deg inside the approved limb. Cells keep their true size, seams are mirror lines. Frame 90 never reaches a fold.
- Limb: the band from 2.2 to 1.2 deg below the limb and everything above it is taken from the plate by elevation and azimuth relative to the current limb (`stLimbDir`), elevation scaled by tan(dip now) / tan(dip approved), so rim, glow and flames follow the limb at any camera height. Below the band the ground is multiplied by T(mu now) / T(mu approved), T measured from the plate: lum 0.48 at mu 0.115, 0.31 at 0.18, 0.24 at 0.275, 0.20 at 0.45, 0.16 above 0.5 (fit 0.15 + 0.12 exp(-m/0.35) + 0.25 exp(-m/0.05), m = max(mu, 0.1) - 0.1). Colour is constant with mu.
- Fringe comb (regular vertical bars at frames 0 and 179): the stretch and flicker phases ran at up to 1000 rad per rad of azimuth. Now 37, 83, 151 for the stretch and 113 for the flicker.
- Shader trap: a second `float band` in the ground shader made the whole ground vanish without any message. A missing layer means a compile error first.
- Camera now: frame 0 is 1.45 behind and 0.52 above the approved pose, frame 180 is 0.48 ahead and 0.17 below. Frame 0 reads as the whole arcade over the spot on a strongly curved limb (radius 20.6 camera heights).
- Merged main 9f78a4e (engine batch 2, engine hash 05c29d3a1588e95f).

## Terrain view, second pass (04:45 to 05:45 UTC)

- Merged main 3523b45 (batch 3, sky aim) and 2d2161b (batch 4). `skyAim` in `plan.ts` holds the values printed by `npm run -s aim -- --stop sun`. The Sun's hold shares its sky with Mercury's arrival, so Mercury's aim decides it later; a hold sky patch must fade out with its own weight before the hold ends (the engine drops it at the first travel frame).
- Light of the loops on the ground is a gain, not an added layer: `loops-glow.png` holds clip(blur(approved) / blur(clean), 1, 12) - 1 inside the loop mask, off in the sky and in the columns above the feet. The ground shader multiplies the plate by 1 + map * beat. An added glow gave red smears and then a milky haze; the gain is crisp. Frame 90 means after it: left of the spot film 191/114/53 against approved 183/112/54, under the left foot 248/235/181 against 249/234/179.
- The loops layer is a hue-preserving residual (approved * clip((lum approved - lum clean) / lum approved)). Plain differences left coloured specks.
- Limb between the feet: the fitted limb sits about 1 deg above the approved bright limb line there and the mirrored sky fill was greyer than the approved. Fix: the sky card is dimmed inside the loop mask near the limb (`DIM` in `sky.py`) and the sky part of the loops layer is computed against the film's own frame 90 without loops (`assets-src/sun/terrain/base90.jpg`, review tier, `look.loops.gain` 0). Order of a full asset run: `plate.py`, `sky.py`, `export.py`, build, render frame 90 at review with loops gain 0 into `base90.jpg`, `plate.py` again.
- Dark specks in the sky under the arcade: the warm filter that keeps the approved stars out of the loops layer left holes at each approved star. The holes are now filled with the opened residual (`HOLES` 13 px). Stars of the film's own frame are opened out of `base90` above 2 deg (`STARS`), so they sit on top of the glow.
- The upper left sky inside the widened loop mask is the clean take's sky, not the approved sky. Same style, different stars. Accepted.
- G13 at every 15th frame of the terrain segment: 0 percent soft on every frame.
- Motion: the loops carry small packets (`flow`) and large swells that run from the left foot to the right in about 2.4 s (`swell`, off within 0.45 s of the judged frame so frame 90 stays the approved picture).

## Hold rework (from 06:05 UTC), Builder notes 1 to 3

Rule adopted: every hold frame must look as good as frame 360. A motion term that damages a still is wrong, however visible it is. Every term is still zero at holdMid, so frame 360 is the hero.

- First exaggeration failed: a luminance-phase twinkle (difference of two sines, reaches 2) drew contour rings on the globe away from frame 360, and the prominence brightness wave went negative (teal gashes in the upper right prominence at frame 270 and 414).
- Globe now: `cell` twinkle is a difference of value noise at two times (`churn`), clamped at 0.35; boil reach 0.0018, max 0.006; `spinDegPerSecond` 1.6 so the surface visibly turns over the 6 s.
- Corona now: a procedural `stream(angle, ln r, t)` (hash value noise, 40 and 96 cells around, periodic) brightens and dims the rays as it runs outward. The old texture flicker is gone.
- Prominences: the brightness wave is `exp2(surge * strength * run)`, so it cannot go negative. `prominence-flow.png` alpha (position along the strand) is now measured from the outward direction at the foot, so its wrap seam lies inside the disc, and the strength channel fades to zero within 0.03 to 0.12 radii of the foot point, where the angle turns too fast (spoke pattern).
- Phone strip measure (`out/work/sun/change.py`, mean absolute difference between frames 36 apart at 400 px wide): disc about 21, corona about 11, sky about 5 of 255.

Sky card, infinity only (Builder note 2):
- Found: the old card was hero minus the film's own halo outside 1.46 radii. Everything the corona model did not reproduce (ray ends, chevrons at the top and bottom frame edge, grain) stayed in the card and would slide against the Sun, because the camera orbits 6 degrees in the hold (about 225 px of sky shift).
- Now: the card is only the generated empty sky (`out/work/sun/sky-fill-take1.png`, upscaled 2x to `sky-fill-x.png`), with its floor (7 px opening) and its stars (the rest) each mapped by quantiles onto the hero's floor and stars measured beyond 1.9 radii (`orbit/infinity.py`). No hero pixel is in the card.
- The corona carries every ray: hero minus the low-pass of that sky, hero data as far out as the corona is at least about twice the sky (`TRUST` 0.3 of the total), a monotone envelope along the radius beyond 1.25 to 1.42 radii, then the power law. Stray orange Milky Way clouds at 1.34 to 1.58 radii on the right were in the prominence matte; detached pieces now have to start below 1.2 radii.
- The engine's own stars do not show through the card in the hold (checked at one bright engine star: 255 without the card, 15 with it).
- Level pass (`orbit/match.py`): the lens bloom adds a veil that the old card had swallowed. One pass: render frame 360 at review without `level.npy` and `veil.json`, compare floors with the hero in polar form, scale the corona by a smooth factor (6 degrees across, 0.02 in ln r, limits 0.3 to 1.5), and take what the corona cannot absorb out of the card as a function of radius only.

## Hold, second pass (06:47 to 07:10 UTC)

- Merged main 45d1651 (engine batch 6: travel cameras, dust, sky lock, ending; engine hash 704d5d55f51b7b15). 35 tests green before, to run again. `npx tsx assets-src/travel/probe.ts`: `sun.travel` join in at 450 turn 0.035 to 0.035 deg per frame, speed 8.19e-2 to 8.15e-2, departing body in view 450 to 472.
- Corona hides what is behind it: alpha = `hide.amount` (0.92) times smoothstep(0.012, 0.07, luminance of the corona texel), blend One / OneMinusSrcAlpha. Film stars no longer shine through the glare near the limb, as in the hero. The level pass (`match.py`) ran once more after it. Floors film against hero at review: far 0.0319 / 0.0322, 1.5 to 1.9 radii 0.0608 / 0.0589, 1.25 to 1.5 radii 0.1668 / 0.1661, 1.08 to 1.25 radii 0.3481 / 0.3491.
- Corona stream has a slow part (11 cells around) so the six phone frames differ; sway 0.0024 with little change along the radius (2.5 and 4.0 rad over the layer), because at 0.004 with 7 and 11 the rays bent into curls at frames 270 and 449. Rule kept: rays stay straight.
- Phone strip numbers (`change.py`, frames 36 apart, 400 px wide, of 255): disc 19 to 26, corona about 9, sky 4.6.
- White smears on the disc at 0.93 to 0.99 radii (found in a native crop of the upper left limb): the far side fill of the wrap comes from the hero's centre, bright and contrasty, and the shader multiplies the limb by 2. Fixed in `wrap.py` (`level`, `level_at`): the fill takes the hero's low-pass colour and local contrast in a zone around the limb, highlights capped and kept warm. Wrap take 3 is in. Limb at frames 270, 360, 449 checked beside the hero: orange, no smear.
- Sky card stars: density map (hero local star light / card local star light, 160 px Gaussian, beyond 1.6 radii, limits 0.5 to 1.7) so the band has as many stars as the hero. Measured at 1920 at the final tier the stars of the dark field came out 24 percent brighter than the hero's (band equal), so the dark field stars are trimmed by 0.8 (`TRIM`). To verify at final.
- Tools: `npm run seq -- --first A --last B --tier draft` renders a range in one browser (11 s a frame). `out/work/sun/jump.py DIR` prints the difference between neighbours and flags a jump.

## Foreground of frame 0: the apron (07:20 to 08:00 UTC)

- Fault: the near ground of frame 0 (the poster frame) was a smear in both lower corners and along the bottom.
- Cause, measured: not the boil (off, same picture) and not the fold (stretch at most 1.6). The generated wide take around the approved image is only 1672 px wide and not true to the ground, and it covered 71 percent of the ground at frame 0 and 39 percent at frame 60 (`out/work/sun/reach4.ts`). All of that near ground lies inside the frustum of frame 0 for frames 0 to 80.
- Fix: a second painted plate, the apron, projected from the frozen frame 0 camera (`src/stops/sun/apron.json`, written by `npx tsx assets-src/sun/terrain/apron-pose.ts`; it does not follow the plan). `src/stops/sun/apron.ts` gives `apImage`, `apUv`, `apWeight`. Weight = (1 - inside the approved frame, feather 0.04) x near fade (ground distance 3.0 to 3.6 from the point under the frame 0 camera) x (1 - smoothstep(0.08, 0.1, distance outside frame 0)). Frame 90 never sees it.
- `ground.ts`: one `for` loop for the approved plate (w < 1), one for the apron (w > 0). `surface()` holds boil and twinkle for either map. Apron tone = stLimb(now) / stLimb(seen from the frame 0 camera). `look.ground.apron = { amount, cell, calm }`: cell 0.1 (boil cell of the apron; 0.3 bent the near cells into swirls at frame 60), calm 0.
- Asset run (all with `PYTHONPATH=assets-src/sun/terrain`): base = frame 0 at draft, `--diag nograin`, boil reach 0, twinkle 0, apron amount 0, saved as `out/work/sun/apron-base.png`; `apron.py mask`; one generation (take 2 kept, prompt in RECIPE.md); `apron.py register` (ECC affine, cc 0.93); `python3 tools/ml/upscale.py --in out/work/sun/apron-crop.png --out out/work/sun/apron-crop-x2.png --width 5504`; `apron.py build`.
- Green specks at frame 0 (8 pixels with G > R + 10): the channel scaling in `build` (base mean over take mean) pushed near-white glints toward green. Now the scaling fades out above 170 to 245 of 255. Frame 0 after: 0 such pixels.
- Test passed: with the base itself as the apron, frame 0 came back in place.
- Known weak: at frame 60 the lowest 140 rows show apron ground that frame 0 has at rows 733 to 800, enlarged about 2 times. Texel rate there is 1.6 per pixel (G13 fine) but the painted cells are fibrous at that size, softer than the approved plate above them. A fix would be a third plate for that band or an unsharp pass in `apron.py build`.
- Never `git checkout src/stops/sun/look.ts` for temporary edits; edit back by hand.

## Flare rework (08:14 to 08:40 UTC), after READY

- Merged main 48a2bfc (engine batches 8 and 9, all eight stops' folders; engine hash a3d211c425dc0406) as 443c9a0. Frame 90 identical before and after; 0 and 204 differ by 1 level in the sky only; 360 on 2,603 pixels (world pin rounding). My set uses no shared Relief and no haze shell, so shield and trim do not apply. Round 10 runs from that head.
- Fault: the flare was a soft white ball hanging in the air between the feet at 196 to 207 (anchor was a point between the feet lifted toward the crown, core power 2.2).
- Now the flare belongs to the arcade:
  - `loops.ts` `burst`: the loops layer is multiplied by 1 + surge x amount x (floor + (1 - floor) x climb), climb = exp(-along / reach) + other x exp(-(1 - along) / reach), reach grows from 0.03 to 0.55 of the strand with the amount, so the white heat climbs from the feet to the crown. Only texels brighter than `from` (0.03 to 0.18 scene luminance) are lifted: without that the faint residue of the layer came up as blotches and combs beside the feet.
  - Two screen kernels (`flare.ts`, two instances): on the left foot and, 0.12 s later at half gain, on the right foot, both 0.06 above the ground. Core power 1.6, radius 0.006 to 0.42, gain 30; rays amount 1.6.
  - Ground: the loop light gain map is multiplied by 1 + 6 x amount (`look.flare.ground`), so the ground under the arcade lights up.
- Cover colour: the old gold (2.4, 0.68, 0.11) was darker than the white frames around it (mean level 216 at 212, 167 at 216, 216 at 220: two flashes with a yellow card between). Now cover (2.8, 1.05, 0.28), low (2.6, 0.85, 0.19): mean 222 at 212, 192 at 216, 219 at 220.
- In the orbit part the kernel sits on the plan spot and dies into the white active region by frame 237. Frame 247 is clean (amount 0 after 1.0 s).
- Tool: `python3 out/work/sun/strip.py DIR OUT COLS WIDTH frames...` makes a contact strip from a `seq` folder.

## Ground motion that damaged the stills (08:40 to 09:00 UTC)

- Found on frame 60 at final, native crops: the whole ground had a melted marble look (wavy contour lines in every cell), and frame 0 had concentric rings in the near cells. Neither is in the plates.
- Cause 1, the boil: the warp along the luminance gradient was clamped to a fixed length (0.14 of a cell), so its direction flipped across every lane and cell centre and stretched the picture there (about 25 px at 1920 in the near field). Now the push is soft limited (`push /= sqrt(1 + |push|^2 / max^2)`), `boil.reach` 0.25, `boil.max` 0.06. Mean absolute difference to the picture with no boil on frame 60: 19 before, about 10 now. At 0.09 the marble starts to come back.
- Cause 2, the twinkle: its phase was the plate's luminance times 60, so away from frame 90 it drew contour rings inside each cell (the same fault the globe had). Now it is value noise on the ground plane in a third time dimension: `vnoise(g * spread, t * rate) - vnoise(g * spread, 0)` plus one finer octave, `twinkle = { amount 0.5, rate 0.9, spread 4 }`. Cells brighten and dim by about 15 percent, no rings. Two texture reads fewer per pixel.
- Frame 90 is byte identical to before (both terms are zero at the judged frame).
- Still open on frame 60: below row 935 the apron's painted cells are about 2.5 times larger than the approved cells just above them and softer (0.7 real pixels per frame pixel). Next: a plate painted from the frame 60 camera for that band.

Corrections from the Builder, worst first (motion first):
1. SEVERE, rise cover 211 to 222: two sides are not one move. Terrain side skims low over a nearly flat horizon (limb at y 155 to 185 of 270), orbit side is a globe about 280 px wide with its top limb at y 35. Terrain camera must climb and pull back before the cover so limb height and curvature agree on both sides; more frames for the cover.
2. MAJOR, glare 204 to 237: white starburst on the left foot at 204 to 212, flat drifting yellow clouds 214 to 218 (the engine cover), kernel jumps to the disc centre 220 to 229, gone abruptly by 237. Glare must stay locked to the active region and exposure must ramp smoothly.
3. MAJOR, camera 221 to 269 and hold: pitch overshoot (top limb y 35 at 222, 7 at 237, 23 at 261), star drift jerks 245 to 270, pull back stops at 269 then creeps inward over the hold (push -0.03). One eased move, no overshoot, no reversal, no creep.
4. MAJOR, match (R1): frame 90 arch, loop strands and spicule glow brighter than approved, highlights cyan white instead of amber; on 90 and 360 dark lanes, penumbra, spot shadows lifted (flatter, slightly warm).
5. MAJOR, near field of terrain (0, 60, 90): soft amber blobs along the bottom, diagonal streaks and whorls with a glossy molten look, radial pinch round a featureless black spot (frame 60 crop A), dotted tracks and cross marks (a mesh) inside the main arch (frame 0 crop H). Frame 90 bottom strip y 866 to 1080 soft with streaks toward the corners. Frame 90 middle ground reads glossy, wet, cells repeat.
6. MAJOR, rise 247: stars in the lower and left sky are short diagonal dashes while the upper sky has sharp points; corona under the lower limb is a brushy mass of vertical strokes ending in grey haze.
7. MINOR: mirror seams in the star sky 245 to 449 (card padded by reflection; outside the border fade into the film's sky, inside nothing may change; `python3 tools/assets/mirrorcheck.py` on main); un-eased start at frame 0 and the reversal of travel 188 to 204; uniform comb spicule fringe with a doubled glow band; one curl motif repeated over the disc; prominence ribbons hard edged and saturated red at 247; loops read as steel wool spark trails; soft round star dots at frame 0. R1 one to two pixel offsets are not to be chased.

Engine facts read for the rise (src/director/moves.ts): cover window fixed by config (208 to 223, centre 216). Before the centre the pose is `terrain(time)` plus exitHeight, exitForward, exitPitchDeg times g = ramp(0, 0.489, u)^2.2 (0.643 at the centre). After it: from = spot + normal x dive.altitude + heading x ahead, e = 1 - (1 - x)^settle, altitude grows geometrically in e, vfov mix(55, 30, smooth(e)), with `rise.level` the horizon is carried (limb `horizonDeg - exitPitchDeg x 0.643` degrees above the frame centre) and goes to the hold framing with smooth(e); blend into the hold pose over u 0.6 to 1. The set's `world` group is placed by the engine against a camera at the origin, so my `terrain` group can carry its own extra transform (a camera path of my own on the terrain side) and the orbit group can be drawn inside the terrain view.

## Rise rebuilt as one path (10:00 UTC on), round 10 items 1 to 3

- `plan.ts` is now `class SunPlan extends StopPlan` with its own `rise()`. One path in globe space from the end of the terrain glide to the hold pose; the terrain view shows it through `local()` (globe to stage: rotation `toStage`, scale `stage.radius`, centre `stage.centre`), the orbit view shows it as it is. The view switches at `switchTime` (cover centre, frame 216). `coverAmount()` returns 1e-4 inside the window so the engine cloud pass draws nothing but the stop still counts as covered.
- `climb` in `plan.ts`: height geometric in e, e from a table of an asymmetric Gaussian rate (peak at x 0.4), vfov mix(55, 30, e^3), the top limb's height in the frame eased from its value at 180 to the hold's, which fixes the pitch. Blend from the glide over x 0 to 0.14 and into `hold()` over 0.72 to 1. `hold.push` 0 (no creep).
- Probe `npx tsx out/work/sun/path.ts`: limb top y (share of frame height) 180 0.485, 204 0.369, 212 0.208, 216 0.144, 270 0.084, monotone. Height 0.72 units at 180, 5.29 units at 215 (0.256 R), 0.304 R at 216, 1.07 R at 224, 3.57 R at 270.
- `look.ground.cap` 63 degrees (was 42), rings 252: from 5.3 units up the limb is 37 degrees away.
- Flare off, frames 184 to 269 (`out/work/sun/raw1-a.jpg`, `raw1-b.jpg`): limb height and curvature agree on both sides of 216. Seen from high up the stage has concentric streaks beyond the painted plates (212 to 215) and the loops layer shows as a milky rectangle (210 to 215). The surface at 215 (granulation, one spot) and 216 (hero swirls) differ, so the veil must be opaque at the switch.

Blend One / OneMinusSrcAlpha: a gold veil with a cover alpha (rises first near the anchor, last at the frame edge) plus a wide halo and a hot core that only comes in near the peak (amount cubed). Anchor: midpoint of the loop feet lifted 0.12 in the terrain view, the plan spot on the globe in the orbit view.

## Rise path, second version, and the grade (10:10 to 10:40 UTC)

- `path(x)` has no blends any more. Start reading `a` = the terrain glide carried on past frame 180 (`coast` 4 s time constant, so near constant speed), end reading `b` = `hold()` at the current time (a moving target, so the orbit rate never dips). A reading is (out, height, level, limb, vfov, rest): `rest` is what is left of the pose's rotation after the frame built from the other five, so the path meets both ends exactly. Height geometric in e, ground track slerp by the linear share of height, vfov mix by e^3, top limb by 1 - (1 - e)^3, `a.rest` out over e 0 to `level`, `b.rest` in with smooth(e).
- `climb.early` 0 (no pedestal), `before` 0.11, `ends` [0.06, 0.25]. Lesson: the mean speed of ground points on the screen must dip at the turn from approach to retreat (forward flow and climb flow oppose), so do not tune for a monotone speed. Tune the acceleration: `npx tsx out/work/sun/flowq.ts A B` prints speed/acceleration per frame in px at 1080. Now the acceleration grows smoothly from 0.02 (180) to 0.84 (192) to 5.4 (206); with a pedestal or a wide `before` it had a bump at 184. Speed 5.9 at 180, lowest 3.4 at 192, 53.6 at 211, 47 at 216, 13 at 232.
- `motion.ts 180 276 4`: distance 1.0349, 1.0345 (188, the glide's own slight descent), then monotone to 4.5741 at 264; limb top y 0.4851 (180), 0.4304 (200), 0.2098 (212), 0.1447 (216), 0.0898 (224), 0.0820 (240), 0.0836 from 260 (0.0016 under, 1.7 px at 1080); orbit rate peak 0.45 deg per frame at 232, 0.0303 at 252, 0.0333 from 264.
- `terrain()` override: time warp over the first 3 s (first version `climb.ease = { over 3, ramp 0.17 }`, replaced at 10:38, see the next section).
- Flare: one kernel on the left loop foot lifted `look.flare.lift`; on the orbit side the same point carried into globe space (`kernelBody` in `set.ts`), so it does not jump at 216. `look.flare.ground` 3 (6 fogged the umbra at 204). Levels at draft with the first path version: mean 104 (204), 135 (208), 155 (210), 190 (212), 203 to 206 (213 to 218), 201 (220), 181 (222), 161 (224), 123 (228); no white clipping.
- Grade (item 4): `src/stops/sun/grade.ts`, a 256 x 4 LUT `grade.png` in sRGB coding applied to the picture value before the inverse film curve: row 0 ground (plate, apron, limb band), row 1 loops, row 2 globe. `assets-src/sun/grade/grade.py` fits it per channel by histogram match of a final nograin render against the approved image (`ground|loops|globe FILM APPROVED lo hi limit`, composes into `out/work/sun/grade.npy`, then `export`; `reset` writes identity). Two passes done. After pass 1 (percentiles 1, 5, 10, 25 of red, film against approved): ground 30/28, 58/58, 74/74, 107/108 (before: about 38/28, 70/58, 85/74, 117/108); globe 83/93, 118/125, 136/141 (before 109/93, 136/125, 151/141; pass 1 overshot, pass 2 takes half back); loops region blue p50 30/24 before pass 2.
- `out/work/sun/gradecheck.py REGION FILM APPROVED` prints the percentiles without changing the table.

## Terrain speed, loop lanes, cover shape, sky lock (10:38 to 11:00 UTC)

- Frame 90's soft bottom strip with streaks to the corners (item 5) is motion blur, not the plate: ground points at the bottom corners moved 10.9 px a frame at 90 and 27 px at 175 with a 180 degree shutter. New time warp in `plan.ts` `terrain()`: speed = settle x smootherstep(x) + gain x sin^2(pi x (min(x / span, 1))^skew) over the first 3 s (`climb.ease = { over 3, settle 0.55, skew 0.6, span 0.8 }`), then constant speed 0.55 after frame 90. Frame 0 and frame 90 poses unchanged, the pose at 180 is earlier on the glide. `npx tsx out/work/sun/corner.ts FRAMES` (px a frame at the bottom left corner): 0.2 (0), 5.4 (10), 10.6 (30, the swoop), 5.4 (60), 6.0 (90), 7.2 (120), 10.4 (178).
- Mesh inside the arch at frame 0 (item 5): not geometry. The flow packets ran in lanes of constant `arc`, one independent noise row every 0.75 degrees, and those lanes lie flat between the feet where the strands hang down, so they drew scan lines, dotted tracks and cross marks. Fix in `loops.ts`: `uFlowMask`, `strand = (1 - smoothstep(inside)) x smoothstep(from, luminance)` multiplies flow and swell (no flow on faint texels or inside the arch), lanes 0.12, the dimming side of the wave is soft limited (`floor` 0.25). Viewed at 0, 60, 150: interior clean, flow still shows on the outer arcs (`out/work/sun/i5-mesh-f.png`).
- Rise after the warp change (`flowq.ts 176 232`, `motion.ts 180 276 4`): speed/acceleration 2.8/0.01 (180), 1.9/0.49 (192, the turn), 11.0/2.2 (200), 39.8/4.9 (212), 38.4/5.3 (216), 12.1/1.8 (232). Distance 1.0398 (180), 1.0395 (184, 188), then monotone to 4.5741 at 264. Limb top y 0.4721 (180), 0.3628 (204), 0.2057 (212), 0.1427 (216), 0.0895 (224), 0.0820 (240), 0.0836 from 260. Orbit rate 0.41 deg a frame at 232, 0.0296 at 252, 0.0333 from 264.
- Draft sequence 176 to 272 (`out/work/sun/rise9-a.jpg`, `rise9-b.jpg`): one move, no jump (neighbour difference 0.9 at 269 to 272). Flat gold from 213 to 219.
- Cover shape (`flare.ts` `cover`, `look.flare.cover = { rise [-0.45, -0.035], fall [0.02, 0.45], power 2 }`): the veil now rises over 12 frames and thins over 13, opaque only round the switch. Mean level at draft: 127 (206), 152 (208), 179 (210), 190, 199, 205 (213), 207 to 208 (214 to 217), 207 (218), 205, 201, 196, 189 (222), 169 (224), 146 (226), 124 (228). Limb seen through the veil to 213, disc from 219 (`out/work/sun/cover1.jpg`).
- Sky lock (item 6, dashed stars at 247): the two sky cards are world meshes and were drawn at each sample's own camera, the engine's stars at the middle of the shutter. Now the cards are on the sky's clock without an engine flag: `plan.seen(kind, time)` gives the local pose for terrain, rise and hold; `set.ts` passes the camera space rotation `seen(mid)^-1 x seen(sample)` and the ratio of the two lens tangents to `skycard.ts` (`uLock`, `uZoom`). Identity at draft (one sample) and in the travel. The camera drift of the director is not in it (far below a pixel).

## Dark specks near the limb, three causes (11:30 to 11:50 UTC)

- Rendering `base90.jpg` again did not remove the dark specks under the arcade at frame 90. Probed pixel by pixel (approved, film, base):
  1. Where the approved image has a white star within about 2 deg of the limb, the loops layer keeps the star out and fills its place with `under`, an opening of the layer. An opening is the lower envelope, so the fill was 8 to 10 levels under its surroundings. Now `under` is at least the warm weighted mean of the layer round the star (`MEND` 8 px, only where at least 20 to 50 percent of the neighbourhood is warm). Speck at (505, 471) gone: film 47 to 50, was 35 to 40.
  2. Where the film's own base has a star below 2 deg, the layer had a hole (approved minus base, clamped), and the layer sits 1 to 2 px off the card, so the hole showed beside the star as a dark dot (571, 480; 1172, 470; 1067, 467). The base's stars were only opened out above 2 deg, to keep the thin spicules of the fringe. Now white small features of the base are opened out from 0.1 to 0.4 deg up (`WHITE`), orange ones stay.
  3. `sky.py` `STARS` 0.4: the card's stars are taken down to 0.4 where the floor is taken down (near the limb), since the approved stars there are drowned in the glow.
- Order when the sky card changes stays: `sky.py`, loops gain 0, build, frame 90 at review into `base90.jpg`, gain 1, `plate.py`, `sky.py`, build (`out/work/sun/chain.sh`).
- `npm run measure -- --stop sun` takes about 25 min here (G13 on 434 frames, 3.5 s each).

Wanted: "at the swap frames the relief view and the globe view must show the same thing, the limb arc at the same height with the same curvature, the sunspot region at the same place, the same exposure and color, the same direction of travel (up and back). So: let the relief camera climb and tilt until its horizon bows like the globe's limb at that height, start the globe view from that exact framing, dissolve over a few frames, then ease the pullback to the hold. Glare is allowed only as a partial veil bound to a footpoint that moves with the camera; the limb stays readable in every frame (as a guide, limb contrast never under a third of what frame 204 has)." Item 1, rise as a matched dissolve (no whiteout):
- The view switches to orbit at the first frame of the window (208, `plan.switchTime = window[0]`). `plan.blend(time)` = x, 0 at 208, 1 at 224, not clamped. While x < 1 the `terrain` group is drawn inside the orbit view (`quaternion = plan.toGlobe`, `scale = 1 / stage.radius`, `position = -toGlobe(stage.centre) / radius`), so relief and globe share one camera: same limb, same place of the spot, same travel.
- Ground alpha in the mixed view: `weight x (1 - fold x smoothstep(ST_FOLD.w - 1.6, ST_FOLD.w, ring)) x (1 - smoothstep(reach - feather, reach, distance from the sunspot)) x facing`, depth test off. `look.dissolve`: far ground out over x 0 to 0.36 (frames 208 to 214), reach 14 to 3.4 units round the spot over x 0 to 0.5, all of it out over x 0.4 to 0.75; fringe and terrain sky out by x 0.4 and 0.5, corona and prominences in over x 0 to 0.7; loops out over x 0 to 0.26 (later the loop cards are seen edge on from above as a thin vertical line: 212 to 214 with [0.05, 0.6]).
- Globe tint and corona dim during the dissolve (`match`, `tint`, `glow`): `uTint` multiplies the display value before the inverse film curve. As an HDR factor it gave cyan and purple patches on near white pixels.
- `flare.ts` is a partial veil now: one kernel sized in world units on the left loop foot, `cover = veil x amount x halo`, veil 0.35, core gain 1.3, halo 0.4, 0.28 s either side of frame 208 + 0.03 s. No engine cover (1e-4), no whiteout.

- Found at 2.2 times gain on review frames 247 and 270: still a faint straight line at the right border of the approved picture (x 1752 at 247, x 1794 at 270, where it was plain to see from y 0 to 450). Two causes, both in `orbit/sky.py`:
  1. The rim lift used one gain a column for all rows, but the rim of the approved image is not equally dark along its length. In bands of rows the lifted rim stood up to 23 percent (left) and 14 percent (right) brighter than columns 7 to 14 inside. Now the lift is the same column gains times a strength for each row (least squares fit of the six columns' star free floor against columns 8 to 40, Gaussian 40 rows, limits 0 to 1.8). Lifted, never lowered. After: at most 9 percent, 4.4 and 4.5 percent rms.
  2. The margin's first columns stood 9 to 12 percent rms (up to 22) off the hero's last columns in bands of rows, because the seam gain compares 48 columns either side and the far side's fine detail was taken from the hero's own rim columns. Now a tight pass (4 columns either side, fading out with 12 px, `TIGHT`) and the far detail from 16 columns in (`INSET`). After: 3.3 percent rms right, 2.1 left (`out/work/sun/seam2.py CARD...`).

## Round 24 result and Phase 6 polish list (mail 34, 21:05 UTC)

Round 24 (my third and last round, judged on main at 530168f): R1 9, R3 5 (parts 5, 7, 7), R4 6, R2 pass (2 of 10 against 2 of 10 for the approved images), R8 pass, measures green, no severe, 11 majors. The pass bar does not hold. Round 24's build ships from Phase 4. Nothing of mine goes onto main before Phase 6. Summary `scorecards/round-024/SUMMARY.md`, open items 46 to 53 in `kit/PROGRESS.md`. agent/sun was fast forwarded to main cb0be39 at 21:05.

Phase 6 polish list from the Builder, worst first, "Work it on agent/sun in this order, motion first":
1. Rise cover, 196 to 222 (R4, two majors). "The sunspot and its immediate rim tighten and streak toward a single point faster than the surrounding surface recedes, making the cover read as a radial warp rather than a camera pullback." and "The tall arch is still fully visible and bright at frame 204 but is already gone as a distinct silhouette when the consecutive sheet starts at 211." Wanted: "the sunspot and its filaments held at one world position and scale through the dissolve so they recede with the surface round them; the arch carried through 204 to 216 as a shape that falls away with the limb, not faded to nothing inside seven frames; exposure matched frame to frame. Proof: a review sheet of every frame 200 to 224."
2. Nearest surface, terrain 0 to 90 (R3, two majors; third round that writes this, "so change technique, do not tune"). "near-camera solar granulation is visibly soft, blurry, and radially smeared at native 1:1 size compared with the crisp mid-ground surface" and "the closest granules are soft and diagonally smeared, with none of the lane and bright-point detail the midground still has". Wanted: "the foreground third built in true 3D, or a detail layer generated at the pixel scale of the frame with lanes and bright points; no stretch toward the lower corners. Proof: native size crops of the bottom strip at 0, 60, 90 beside the middle ground."
3. Far photosphere under the limb at 60 (R3 major). "bilateral mirror-symmetric butterfly seams repeat vertically in the solar granulation texture just below the horizon, exposing mirrored UV tiling". The critic's crop: x 732, y 324, 456 by 433 of frame 60 scaled to 1400 px wide; also seen at 0 and 90. Wanted: "find it at native size, point mirrorcheck at the ground layers, replace any reflected fill by granulation that does not repeat."
4. Limb and sky, terrain 0 and 60 (R3, two majors). "the orange chromospheric glow terminates in a hard, dark-rimmed curved line and doubled shell edge parallel to the limb instead of fading smoothly into space" and "a ruler-straight horizontal bright line cuts across the base of the left coronal loop footpoint and extends into the sky like an unmasked flare or card edge". Wanted: "the glow feathered into the sky with no shell edge on every terrain frame; the straight line traced to its layer (flare veil, loop card or sky card edge) and removed. Proof: crops at 3 times gain at 0, 30, 60, 90."
5. Granulation as a material, 247 and 360 (R3, two majors). "granulation reads as glossy poured fluid, and the flow pinches into the pores instead of forming discrete cells" and "the granulation is one busy, even, painterly noise everywhere, with no change in scale toward the limb. The sunspots all share the same starburst template, and the surface reads as over-sharpened illustration rather than photosphere". Wanted: "finer cells with dark lanes toward the limb generated into the map with the approved pixels kept as the low frequencies, the flow kept out of the pores. R1 at 360 is 9, so every change is checked against the approved hero and must not cost R1."
6. Corona at 360 (R3 major). "the corona is a fan of straight, brush-stroke streaks of near-equal length and spacing all round the disc, airbrushed in texture, and some streaks run in front of the star field like paint". Wanted: "outside the approved frame only, streamers varied in length, spacing and falloff, faint and additive over the stars. Inside the approved frame the corona is the approved image's own and stays."
7. Minors, after the majors: loop feet bloom smaller and dimmer than approved and the grade about 2 levels redder at 90; a jagged pure black ribbon between the warm glow and the stars, and stretched corner stars, at frame 0; a wet highlight on the cell crowns at 90; the arch keeps one filament pattern from 0 to 98 (a slow run of light along its strands).
Not mine to cure: the R4 critic's request for a dive sheet (the Sun has no dive).

Rules: "only your three folders; the judged frames 0, 60, 90, 247, 360 stay on their poses; every change proved with the harness tools (measure, mirrorcheck, samples) and looked at on stills; merge origin/main before each push; engine wishes go into assets-src/sun/ENGINE-REQUESTS.md. When items 1 to 3 hold on your VM, send "POLISH READY <commit>" with what changed per item and the proof pictures on agent/sun-looks, then go on with 4 to 7. Phase 6 has at most three polish rounds for the whole film, so an item is sent when it is right, not when it is new."

## Polish study, items 1 to 3 (21:06 to 21:30 UTC)

Measured on the round 24 tree (4081281), nothing changed yet:
- Sizes: stage radius 20.6267 units (1 unit = 0.0485 R). Sunspot at stage (0.107, -0.278, -2.448), ring 2.10 from the down point. Relief umbra half width 0.28 units (0.0138 R). Loop feet 1.33 and 1.44 units from the spot, 2.08 apart. `out/work/sun/spotsize.ts`.
- Rise per frame (distance d, px a unit at the site, incidence in degrees): 204 1.0735, 460, 50; 208 1.1165, 355, 38; 212 1.2061, 231, 26; 214 1.2805, 178, 21; 216 1.3823, 137, 18; 220 1.6924, 83, 14; 224 2.1623, 55, 13; 247 4.4895, 27.
- Item 3, cause found (`out/work/sun/foldwhere.ts`): the plate is reflected at ring `ST_FOLD.w` 3.599, at the near edge 0.657 and at phi 0.905 either side (`stFold` in `ground.ts`, plate and apron loaded with `mirror: true`). At frame 60 the fold ring projects at y 521, the limb band starts at y 514 (full at 496), the limb is at y 474: a reflected strip of 15 to 20 px lies under the limb band. Frame 90: ring y 532, band 524 to 506. Frame 0: ring y 505, band 499 to 481. From above (draft 208 to 222 with the relief kept at full weight, `i155-p1-relief-full.jpg`) the far ground shows mirrored sunspots and concentric streaks: the far relief is not fit to be seen from above as it is.
- Item 2: the bottom strip of frame 90 equals the approved image, whose own near cells are soft and stretched toward the corners (`i157`). So nothing can be won by resampling: the detail has to be generated.
- Item 1, causes: (a) the ground alpha in the mixed view shrinks (`reach` 14 to 3.4 units) while the camera pulls back, so the relief's edge closes on the sunspot faster than the surface recedes: this is the radial warp; (b) the loop cards go edge on at 213/214 (outer lean +22 degrees faces the rising camera) and the inner sheet (lean -28) shows as a translucent rectangle with rain streaks; (c) `loops.burst.surge` 26 times `flare.loops` 0.5 whitens the arch at 206 to 210.

Design decided (one technique for items 3 and 1, then item 2):
1. The field. `assets-src/sun/terrain/field.py` rectifies plate and apron top down into flat ground coordinates at 320 px a unit (3 times supersampled), with the sunspot and the feet masked out, limb tone divided out, and quilts a square tile from the valid patches (Efros and Freeman, minimum cut seams). `ground.ts` samples the tile where the plate and the apron have no picture of their own (beyond ring 3.0 to 3.6, beyond phi, at the near edge), by stochastic tiling (triangle grid, hashed offsets), with the limb tone of the current view. `stFold` and the mirrored wraps go.
2. Rise. Ground alpha in two world fixed zones with uniform fades and no moving edge: a fixed disc round the sunspot fading about 214 to 223, the far zone about 208 to 215. Arch: lean animated so no card is edge on, gradual fade 206 to 220, the white surge cut to a steady lift, exposure matched frame to frame.
3. Near ground: generated detail pass on the plate's foreground third and on the apron, high frequencies of the take over the low frequencies of the approved plate.

- 21:24: `field.py ortho` works: `out/work/sun/field/ortho-plate.npy` and `ortho-apron.npy` (2944 x 2240 at 320 px a unit, scene values at limb tone 1), valid share 2.9 percent (plate, two wedges either side of the sunspot, each about 300 x 500 px) and 6.9 percent (apron, one crescent about 1160 x 400 px). Pictures `i158-ortho-plate.jpg`, `i158-ortho-apron.jpg`.

## Polish, item 3 built: the field (21:30 to 21:45 UTC)

- `assets-src/sun/terrain/field.py` (`ortho`, `quilt`, `finish`; run with `PYTHONPATH=assets-src/sun/terrain`). `ortho` rectifies plate and apron top down at 320 px a unit (3 times supersampled), grade row 0, limb tone normalised to a view 0.25 from grazing, masks away the sunspot (1.35 units), the feet (0.7) and everything reflected. `quilt` is image quilting (Efros and Freeman): 192 px blocks, 64 px overlap, tolerance 0.12, use penalty 3.0, minimum error paths on all four sides, toroidal, seed 20261121. Source is the apron crescent only (1404 x 576 px): with the plate's two wedges as a second source their orange highlights came back as a repeating blob. `finish` flattens 75 percent of the swell wider than 220 px and writes `public/assets/sun/full/field.jpg` (4096, 10.6 MB), `web/field.jpg` (2048, 3.4 MB), `src/stops/sun/field.json` (size 12.8 units, tone 0.25). Recipe written.
- `ground.ts`: `stFold` is gone (also out of `stage.ts`). `fieldAt`: triangle grid of 1.6 units, each corner reads its own window of the tile at a hashed place kept inside the tile (the engine's repeat wraps S only), three taps, weights to the power 6, contrast put back (Heitz and Neyret). Plate weight `own` = ring fade [3.6, 4] times phi feather 0.1 times near feather 0.3; the field shows with `(1 - own) * (1 - apron weight)`. The apron no longer reads beyond its own picture (`apron.ts`, `within`).
- `look.ground.band` is [3.4, 2.6] (was [2.2, 1.2]): at pose 90 the limb band covers everything beyond ring 3.6, so the field has no weight in the judged frame 90.
- GLSL: `flat`, `open`, `low` cannot be names.
- Draft checks: mirrorcheck clean on 0, 60, 90, 150, 204, 208 and on the tile; texel view at draft (the measure's own tier and size, 1920 wide): no soft pixel at 0, 90, 179, 190, 200, 204, 207, lowest 1.72. Pictures `i160` to `i163`.
- Still to prove at the final tier: 0, 60, 90 beside round 24, frame 90 against the approved image.

## Polish, item 1: the rise cover (21:39 UTC on)

What the picture needed, found by looking, in this order:
1. The ground's alpha had a shrinking reach (14 to 3.4 units). Gone: `Ground.show(mixed, far, site)` is two plain weights, nothing moves on the ground.
2. Sunspot size. My study note said the hero's sunspot is 0.65 of the relief's. Looked at properly (site locked crops at one world scale, 3.2 units a side, frames 208 to 224, `i168-site-same-scale.jpg`) the two are the same size within what the eye can tell: dark core about 0.55 units, filaments to about 0.8. So no scale trick is needed and none is built. The crop strip at one world scale is the proof picture for "one world position and scale".
3. Arch: `loops.ts` has a second vertex set (`aLate`, leans -24 and -42 degrees) mixed in by `uLate` from frame 190 to 212.5, so no card is edge on to the rising camera; the white surge is cut (`look.flare.loops` 0.15, was 0.5); the cards' cut grows with `late` (`look.dissolve.cut` 0.12) so the inner sheet does not show as a pane. The arch is a shape in every draft frame 204 to 216.
4. From above the plate's far ground (ring 2.4 to 4) is 17 to 35 percent brighter and redder than the ground round the sunspot and than the field (ring profile from `ortho-plate.npy`: luminance 0.19 at ring 1, 0.22 at 2, 0.28 at 3.4, 0.31 at 3.9; apron and field 0.23), and soft. At 209 to 213 that showed as a straight line through the feet where the field takes over. Cure in `ground.ts`, by view: `lift = smoothstep(0.1, 0.4, now - graze)` is how much steeper the film camera looks at a ground point than the plate's camera did. It is 0 on every terrain frame for ring 2 and beyond (checked by hand: at 179 the difference is 0.05) and exactly 0 at pose 90. With lift: the plate's far tone is evened (`look.ground.even`), its own zone ends earlier and on a rough edge (`own.up` [3, 3.9], `own.rough`), and the loops' ground glow, which is laid out in plate picture coordinates and from above ran as a bright column from the arch to the limb, ends at ring 3.5 to 4.3 (`glowReach`).
5. Two zones, both fixed to the world: within 1.1 units of the sunspot (feather to 2.8) the relief stays until x 0.45 and is gone at x 1 (frames 215 to 224); the rest goes x 0.1 to 0.75 (frames 210 to 220). x is `plan.blend`, 0 at frame 208 and 1 at 224.
- Draft renders: `q2` (first pass), `q3` (cut, later loops fade), `q4` (two zones, lift). Mean level per frame in `q3`: 74 at 200, 90 at 206 to 210 (the flare), 108 at 217, 98 at 224, largest step 4.5 levels a frame at 203 to 205, no step out of line (`jump.py`).

## Polish, item 2: generated foreground (22:11 UTC on)

- Where the picture comes from: at frame 0 the approved frame's bottom edge lies at y 919 to 898 (corners x 343 and 1714), at frame 60 at y 1004 to 990 (corners x 170 and 1815). So the bottom strip of frame 0 is mostly apron, at 60 only the last 80 px, at 90 none. The diagonal smear in the lower right of 60 and 90 is the approved image's own wide lens stretch and blur (`i179-rf7-bottom-0-60-90.jpg`).
- Lesson: a take made at about one model pixel a screen pixel (the whole frame in one picture, 1.43) comes back crisp with lanes. Tiles at 3.9 model pixels a screen pixel come back as large smooth pebbles. So: whole frame takes, not tiles.
- Plate takes, all six used (`out/work/sun/detail/`): R-1, R-2, R-3 (the right tile at 3.9 model px a screen px: flat pale cells, glossy pebbles, not used); W-1 (the whole approved image, foreground only sharpened: same layout, crisp lanes, lower right cells still long); W-2 (W-1 with the lower right corner greyed out and painted new: compact cells, but giant ones at the bottom right); W-3 (same input, with the size sentence of round 11: compact cells of the neighbours' size in the whole corner). W-3 is kept. Pictures `i188`, `i192`, `i194`, `i195-take-W-3-crops.png`, `i196-take-W-3-agree.png`.
- W-3 against the approved image (`detail.py place`): ECC affine cc 0.9973, band agreement 3 to 12 px and 12 to 48 px 0.93 to 1.0 everywhere outside the greyed corner, about 0 inside it (new cells, as wanted).
- `detail.py compose`: take lower part upscaled 2 times, warped into plate coordinates, used only in the foreground (v from 0.635 to 0.705 down, kept away from the sunspot's filaments by an ellipse), as take x low(base) / low(take): the approved plate stays as the low frequencies (sigma 150 plate px, 520 in the new corner, where the long cells themselves have to go).
- Result of the W-3 compose in the film (drafts `out/work/sun/d1`): the lower right corner has compact cells without smear; left and centre foreground hardly change, because the model kept the soft layout where it only sharpened. Fine band of the plate itself (mean absolute difference of Gaussians 0.7/1.5 px): bottom left 3.71 to 4.01, bottom right 5.09 to 4.86, middle 8.2. Not enough.
- Tried and dropped: line integral convolution veins and bright points baked into the plate (`out/work/sun/veins_proto.py`, `i209`, `i210`): reads as fur and starbursts.
- Lesson: "sharpen only" edits keep the softness. Only areas painted new from grey change character. Whole frame takes (1.43 model px a screen px) give crisp lanes.

## Polish, item 2: the apron as the foreground plate (22:35 UTC on)

- Decision: the apron becomes the foreground picture also INSIDE the approved frame's foreground third (the "front" zone), painted new from grey in a whole frame take of frame 0, because the approved image's own near cells are soft and stretched and three rounds have faulted them. The approved pixels stay above the front line and as the low frequencies (tone) below it.
- `src/stops/sun/apron.json` is re-posed to the current frame 0 (`assets-src/sun/terrain/apron-pose.ts`; the old pose was the old frame 0, 1.25 units behind the approved pose) and has `front: { side, centre, at, span, feather }`: the front line in approved image coordinates (v from the top), `side` at the sides, `centre` under the sunspot (so the penumbra filaments stay approved), blended over `span` of |x - at|. `apron.ts` `apWeight`: `front = smoothstep(line - feather, line, a.y)`, weight `max(1 - inside, front) * max(near, front * inside) * within`. The blend band lies just above the line, where the take equals the base picture.
- Apron takes (asset `apron.jpg`, 6 allowed this round): A-1, A-2 whole frame 0 "sharpen only" (A-2 built once: cc 0.994, agreement 0.96 to 0.99, only slightly sharper, finest band ratio bottom to middle at 90 0.50 to 0.57, not enough). A-3: foreground greyed (`detail.py apron-grey`, line 0.67 sides, 0.79 centre) and painted new: crisp polygonal cells with dark lanes, fibrils and bright points, right perspective growth toward the camera (cells about 60 px wide at the grey line, about 230 at the bottom edge, in take px), ECC cc 0.998. Built (`apron-A3-full.jpg`) and rendered (`out/work/sun/d4`, `i214-*`): frame 90 bottom strip is crisp, corners compact, no smear. Two faults: (1) at frame 0 the frame edge is the apron picture's edge, so the `within` feather (0.04) shows the soft field in the last 43 rows and 77 columns; (2) cells read a little like flat tiles with thick outlines beside the domes of the middle ground, and under the sunspot (v 0.71 to 0.79) the approved image's own softer cells remain.
- A-4 (in work 23:10): the same, but on a wide canvas: frame 0 at 0.88 of the canvas inside a grey frame (2752 x 1536), so the apron picture is the frame 0 view with a margin (`apron.json` `wide` 0.88, `vfovDeg` 61.21) and the `within` feather lies outside frame 0; front line centre 0.775; wording asks for swollen domes and thin lanes. Base for A-3 and A-4: `out/work/sun/d3off/f00000.png` (frame 0 draft, nograin, boil and twinkle off, W-3 plate, A-2 apron).
- Fallback if A-4 is worse: A-3 with a `within` feather that opens only after frame 100 (by then the apron's edge has left the frame: forward 0.45 units moves it 100 px or more).

So lanes and bright points are new and crisp, cells, colour and tone are the approved image's. The base is the frame 0 draft's lower half enlarged by `tools/ml/upscale.py` (as in round 11), not by cubic. Prototype pictures `i230-*`, `i232-*`, `i233-A-6-mix-c-centre.png`: approved domes with a crisp dark lane network, no tiles, no fur.
- Lesson: a generated foreground alone always changes the material (bubbles, tiles or fur). The approved cells with a generated lane and point layer keep the material.

## Polish, item 2: the lane layer as built (23:35 to 00:05 UTC)

- First build of the lane layer with the base from the old draft (1920 px, cubic): crisp lanes but the base's own softness stayed under them. So the base is now frame 0 rendered at the `uhd` format (3840 x 2160, draft tier, `--diag nograin`, boil and twinkle off, the round 11 apron and its pose in place, front zone off: `out/work/sun/apron.base.json`, `out/work/sun/d7base/f00000.png`), lower half to 5504 x 1548 by Lanczos (`detail.py apron-base`). 1.43 base px a apron px: no enlarger needed.
- `detail.py apron-build` now (constants `APRON_LANES`, `APRON_UNEVEN`, `APRON_VEINS`, `APRON_CROWN`, `APRON_MATCH`, `APRON_HOLD`): e = log luminance of take A-6 minus its Gaussian of 6 screen px (lanes and bright points); where e darkens: gain 0.95, times a smooth random factor 0.45 to 1 (cells of 70 px, seed 20261121, so the network is not evenly strong), times 0.1 on the base's own bright crowns (so the new lanes do not cut through the approved domes); where it brightens: 0.6. Plus the fine veins of take A-5 (log luminance minus Gaussian 2.5 px, gain 0.4, 0.24 where it brightens). out = base x exp(e), darkening tinted red brown; weight rises over 60 screen px below the front line (distance transform of the grey area); highlights held under the base's own 96 px maximum; each channel put back on the base's mean over 40 px.
- Tone pass (`detail.py apron-tone FRAME90`): the apron is multiplied by approved / film at frame 90 in a Gaussian of 40 px (limits 0.75 to 1.3 a pass, fading to 1 over 0.1 of the frame outside the approved border; a lift is halved on pixels above 0.55 to 0.9 so the crowns do not clip). Three passes from scratch (`out/work/sun/tonepass.sh TAG DIR` builds, copies, renders 90 without grain and prints the difference). Frame 90 minus approved in rows 760 to 1080 at draft: -5.6/-3.1/-2.7 before, -0.44/-0.47/-0.54 after (round 24: -1.8/-1.2/-1.1); pixels above 235 there 22938 (approved 21320, round 24 18776). Frame 0 low frequencies against round 24: within 3.2 levels everywhere (first try without the fade outside the border: +23 in the bottom left corner).

## Item 4, cause of the shell edge found offline (01:15 to 01:40 UTC)

- `out/work/sun/limbsky.ts FRAMES`: where the limb stands in the sky (in degrees above the frame 90 limb line, by azimuth -40 to 40): frame 0 -0.87 (left) to -0.28 (right), frame 30 -0.74 to -0.24, frame 60 -0.38 to -0.12, frame 100 +0.13 to +0.04. Camera height 1.180 (0), 1.151 (30), 1.075 (60), 1.000 (90), 0.975 (100) units. 18.1 px a degree.
- The terrain sky card (`sky.py`) is at infinity but carries three structures that follow the frame 90 limb line: (1) its low frequencies are dimmed to nothing below 0.5 degrees above the line and to 0.9 at 1.7 degrees (`NEAR`); (2) from a line 2.1 degrees (inside the approved frame) to 4.6 degrees (outside) above the limb downward it is a reflected fill of the sky above that line; (3) the orange haze above 0.9 to 1.8 degrees stayed in the card (`plate.py` gives only the orange below `NEAR` 0.9 to 1.8 degrees to the plate, where the fringe reads it). At frame 90 the fringe and the analytic glow fill the dimmed band exactly. At frames 0 to 60 the limb stands 0.3 to 0.9 degrees (5 to 16 px) lower, so a dark band opens between the flames and the card's haze, and the haze keeps the shape of the old limb line: this is the "dark-rimmed curved line and doubled shell edge parallel to the limb", and the "pure black ribbon between the warm glow and the stars" of item 7.
- Cure decided (at the source, the rule "the sky card may carry only what is at infinity"): everything that belongs to the limb goes into a limb fixed layer. New texture `haze` in limb coordinates (azimuth by elevation 0 to 8 degrees, star free: analytic glow plus the smoky haze of the approved image), read by `fringe.ts` without flicker, with a soft top. The card keeps stars and a smooth background that runs on through the band and below the limb line with no structure along the limb. At frame 90 the sum is the approved image by construction (haze = approved sky minus new card).
- The straight line at the left foot: it is the approved image's own horizontal lens streak (approved native x 780 to 1130, y 705 to 715), carried by `loops.webp` (card rows about 1140 to 1180, columns 320 to 1020). At 90 the film equals the approved image; at 0 and 60 it shows the same way (`i276-*`). ## Mail 53 from the Builder (01:24 UTC Nov 22): the opening at phone size, wanted by about 03:30 UTC

"One addition to your list, placed after your cure of items 46 to 48 and before the minors. Wanted by about 03:30 UTC if you can, as its own commit with one switch in your plan (off gives today's frames, byte for byte)."

He sees frames 0, 8, 15, 23, 30, 38, 45, 53 and 60, each 400 px wide, and is asked: would this stop someone scrolling with the sound off, is the subject readable in the first frame, does something change inside the first two seconds that rewards staying. Bar: 9 of 10 with no major."
"My look at those nine frames of the kept clip (round 24 build): frame 0 is strong and reads at once (the arch, the spot, the grain of the surface). The change over the two seconds is a slow push (the horizon at mid frame moves 49 of 1080 px between 0 and 60) and strands that differ in detail. At 400 px the nine frames look nearly alike, so the third question is the weak one."
"Wanted: motion that reads at 400 px without changing what frame 90 shows against the approved close image. For example: bright knots of plasma running along the arch from one foot to the other (a travelling brightening you can follow from frame to frame, two or three of them inside the two seconds); the flares at the feet pulsing; the granules nearest the lens visibly boiling (cells brightening and dimming over half a second); the push a little faster in the first second and eased again by frame 60. The kit's line is: 'Something visibly moves inside the first seconds: the surface boils and plasma flows along the loops.' You know the scene; choose what holds up at native size too."
"Proof, on your looks branch: one strip of 0, 15, 30, 45, 60 at 400 px wide with the switch off and one with it on; frames 0, 60 and 90 at the final tier beside the kept stills; frame 90's numbers against the approved close image before and after; your own list of what is weak. No new seam, no new soft area: items 46 to 48 are about this very view, so the motion goes on top of their cure, not in its place."
"Report as POLISH READY with the commit of the switch named, so it can be merged by itself."

## The opening switch, written offline (01:25 to 01:45 UTC), NOT yet in `repo/src`

The code sits in the scratch copy `/home/user/workspace/repo2/src/stops/sun/` (`plan.ts`, `look.ts`, `loops.ts`, `ground.ts`, `set.ts`; `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` passes there). It is copied into `repo/src/stops/sun/` only after the review sheet 200 to 224 has finished. Until then `repo/src` is the committed tree.

- Envelope: `open` = 1 over frames 0 to 61, fading to 0 between 2.05 and 2.85 s after frame 0 (`look.opening.fade`), so frame 86 onward keeps today's bytes. With the switch off the shader text is string identical to today's (checked), so off gives today's frames.
- `plan.ts`: `export const opening = { on, level: 0.9, until: 2 }`. `run(t)` returns `push(t)` while `opening.on && t < until`. `push`: even speed (about 0.555 of cruise) until 0.9 s, smoothstep rise to cruise at 2 s, frame 0 centred on its old pose at mid shutter, pose and speed at frame 60 and after unchanged. Checked with `repo2/out/push.ts`: share of the 0 to 60 travel (0.272 units) at frame 15 old 0.03, new 0.20; at 30 old 0.26, new 0.41; at 45 0.63, 0.66; frames 60, 90, 179 difference 0.
- `look.ts`: `opening = { fade: [2.05, 2.85], knots: { amount: 4, speed: 0.5, every: 0.9, width: 0.045, jitter: 0.25, white: 0.5, inside: [1.9, 2.2], lanes: [4, 1.7], lead: 0.2, pick: [0.25, 0.4, 0.75, 0.1], ends: [0.03, 0.2] }, feet: { amount: 0.6, reach: 0.07, over: 0.14, lane: 1.5, ground: 0.5 }, seethe: { amount: 0.5, rate: 2, spread: 4, near: [2.2, 4.5], far: 0.3, crown: [0.85, 1.25] } }`.
- `loops.ts`: `frag(knots)`; uniforms `uKnot` (amount x open, speed, every, width), `uKnotMore` (since + lead, jitter, white, feet reach), `uKnotLane`, `uKnotPick`, `uKnotEnds`, `uFoot`. Knots run from the left foot to the right at one speed in image plane units along circular lanes (true arc length: s = angle at foot B / (pi - arc), lane length apart x (pi - arc) / sin(arc)), launched every 0.9 s, each knot bright only on some lane bundles (a noise row per knot index), white added. `pulse(since)` gives the two foot beats (launch at the left foot, arrival at the right). `update(..., cut, late, open = 0, since = 0)`.
- `ground.ts`: `frag(seethe)`; uniforms `uSeethe`, `uSeetheNear`, `uCrown`: cell crowns (luminance over wide luminance) brighten and dim by noise in ground coordinates at rate 2 a second, full nearer than 2.2 units, 0.3 beyond 4.5. `update(..., lit, open = 0, since = 0, beat = 0)`, `uGlowBeat` times `(1 + beat)`.
- `set.ts`: in the terrain branch `since = state.time - plan.seg.terrain.start`, `open = opening.on && kind === 'terrain' ? 1 - share(since, look.opening.fade) : 0`, feet from `loops.pulse(since)`, ground beat `look.opening.feet.ground * open * 0.5 * (feet[0] + feet[1])`.

## Item 4, design refined offline (01:35 to 01:45 UTC)

- Prototype `out/work/sun/haze/sky2.py OUTDIR` (a new `sky.py` in the making; writes only under `out/work`). Split: `true` = Gaussian 24 px of the true sky normalised by the open sky mask; `back` = smooth background from the sky above 5 to 6 degrees (normalised blur, sigma 110, 330, 990 px, continued below the limb); `weight` = 1 - step(above, 4.5, 8). New card = (back + (1 - weight)(floor - back) + old star part (out - floor)(0.4 + 0.6 near)) x arch factor: no low frequency of the card follows the limb. Haze texture H(az, el) = analytic veil (values of `assets-src/sun/terrain/glow.json`) + weight x (true - back) x arch factor x open mask, sampled in limb coordinates of pose 90: azimuth -64 to 64 degrees over 4096 px, elevation 0 to 8 degrees over 256 px, el 0 at the bottom row, display coded 8 bit with seeded dither, `haze.png` full and web half size.
- `fringe.ts` is to read H instead of calling `stGlow` (uv = (az / 128 deg + 0.5, el / 8 deg), `az` computed before the glow branch, clamp, no flicker), loaded in `set.ts`, named in RECIPE. At frame 90 the sum equals the approved image by construction.
- Found: `src/stops/sun/terrain.json` glow (a 0.01112, 0.05159; s 1.1898, 1.1897) differs from `assets-src/sun/terrain/glow.json` (a 0.01461, 0.04802; s 1.1643, 1.1644; written 13:58 Nov 21 by `plate.py`, `export.py` not run after): the shader's glow is 2 to 6 percent above the veil that was subtracted. The baked H removes this.
- The loops card is "approved minus base90": after any change of the card or the glow the chain must run again (`out/work/sun/chain.sh`: sky, loops gain 0, build, frame 90 at review into `base90.jpg`, gain 1, `plate.py`, `sky.py`, build), and the plate now also carries the W-3 detail compose (`detail.py compose`), so the order needs care. History: the old `near` dimming exists because the card near the limb made the film brighter than approved by about the whole card.
- Mail 49: do not grade the card toward or away from the shared band while batch 17 is in flight. The new card keeps the old card's values above 8 degrees, so its border against the shared sky does not move.

## Item 4, offline assets ready (01:45 to 01:58 UTC), nothing under `public/` or `src/` touched yet

It writes `OUTDIR/full|web/sky-terrain.webp` (the new card), `OUTDIR/full|web/haze.png` (4096 x 256 and 2048 x 128) and `out/work/sun/haze/haze.json` (azDeg 64, elDeg 8, gain 4, size).
- What was learned from the pieces (`plate.py`): the approved scene is `veil` (analytic glow, the fringe's `stGlow`) + `rem`; `rem` = `flames` (orange below 0.9 to 1.8 degrees, in the plate, added by the fringe above the geometric limb) + sky scene S = (rem - flames)(1 - ground). The ground mask's edge is the top of the bright spicule layer, 0.4 to 1.5 degrees above the geometric limb, with a hump at the left foot. Inside the approved frame the card has been a reflected fill below 2.1 degrees all along, so the true S below 2.1 degrees has never been in the card.
- Final split: card low frequencies = `back` (smooth background from above 5 to 6 degrees, normalised blurs 110, 330, 990 px, pressed down where the wide true sky is darker) + (1 - w)(floor - back), w = 1 - step(above, 2.5, 5.5); star part as before (`(out - floor)(0.4 + 0.6 step(above, 1.7, 4.5))`, fill stars below the line); times the arch factor. H = max(veil x colour + w (blur24(S) - back) x arch, 0), no sharp part and no normalised blur (a first try with the sharp part baked the approved image's stars into H: not allowed, "No baked stars in anything that is not a sky patch"). At pose 90 card + H = veil + blur24(S) wherever the sum is not clipped (clipped mean -0.00008 scene units, worst -0.015 in the hump at the left foot, where the plate is bright anyway).
- Numbers: `back` at the limb line is 15, 14, 13 display levels (17, 16, 16 two degrees below, 21, 19, 18 at 5 degrees above); the old card's low frequencies were 0 below 0.5 degrees, 7 at 1 degree, 21 at 2 degrees: that step was the dark rim. w (S - back) is negative near the limb (-0.008 at 0.25 to 0.5 degrees against a veil of 0.05 to 0.04): the true sky right above the limb is nearly black once veil and flames are taken out, and H carries that as a glow a little under the veil. Beyond the canvas (azimuth past 57.7 degrees) H repeats its last column.
- H coded as display(H x 4), dithered (seed 20261122), zero kept zero; the shader divides by the gain after `inverseFilmic`.

## Mail 63 from the Builder (03:10 UTC Nov 22), to the stop agents, no reply needed

"Engine batch 18 is on main: a67c9c34. Merge it into your branch (`git fetch origin main`, `git merge origin/main`, no rebase), build, tests. It changes nothing in your hold, dive and terrain frames (29 of the 37 judged stop frames keep their bytes at the final tier; Saturn's hold differs in one pixel by one level); your judged rise frame gets a slightly higher sky lift (0.74) and was looked at beside batch 17."
"One finding to check in your own shaders, from the Saturn agent. Frames that do not repeat from one fresh page to the next (one level on a few edge pixels) came from this: the harness GPU shades 2 x 2 pixel quads; a pixel that hit `discard` stops storing to variables, so a local first assigned after the discard holds stale memory, and `dFdx`, `dFdy`, `fwidth` and `texture()` without an explicit level read it for the live neighbours of the quad. Rule: no `discard` before the last statement of a fragment shader (carry a flag and discard at the end), and no derivative or implicit level `texture()` on a value first assigned inside a per pixel branch. If you find the pattern in your folder, fix it with your next commit and say in your next READY which frames changed and by how many pixels. G3 asks for pixel identity across fresh page loads."

- 03:10 merged origin/main 0622510 (holds batch 18, a67c9c34) as c4487b0, with the item 4 work uncommitted in the tree. Build passes. STOP-AGENT.md gained four paragraphs: `coverHidden(segment, time)`, `skyTurnEase: { dive, rise }` (eases the sky turn over the cover window instead of the switch at the centre), `light.glareCarry`, and "a cover pass that cannot reach a pixel is not drawn". None set by me. To check on the next every frame sheet: whether the star field jumps at the centre of my rise cover (then `skyTurnEase.rise`).
- Shader check for the Saturn finding (all of `src/stops/sun/*.ts`): every `discard` is `if (uDiag >= 2) discard;` on a uniform (corona, flare, fringe, loops, prominence, skycard), so a draw discards every pixel or none and no quad has a live neighbour of a discarded pixel. Derivatives: corona (u, u2, v from varyings, unconditional), globe (uv0 unconditional; g0 inside `if (uPatchCount > 0.5)`, a uniform), ground (uvF, uv, uvB unconditional), loops, prominence, skycard (varyings). Implicit level `texture()`: prominence (uFlow at uv and p, unconditional) and skycard (unconditional). The per pixel branches (`for (int k = 0; k < (cond ? 1 : 0); k++)`) hold only `textureLod` and `textureGrad`. So the pattern is not in my folder; nothing changed. Evidence so far: draft and final frames repeated byte for byte across separate page loads many times (d13, o1off, o3off, rf12, rf14).
