## Item 4, the cure rebuilt on the clean canvas (03:36 to 03:45 UTC)

- The second trial of `toward()` was wrong in its target: "approved minus loops card" is circular (the loops card is the positive part of approved minus base90, so every place where the base is too dark was already cut out of the need, and the move could only lower the haze). New target, no circle: the clean canvas itself. `plate.py` now saves `out/work/sun/clean-half.npy` (the scene values `s` of the canvas at half size, the very picture the plate, the veil and the sky scene are cut from). `s` does not depend on `base90.jpg` (the clean picture is `da + (dc - da) x wide_m` from the untouched takes), so the haze does not depend on the loops card.
- `toward()` in `sky.py`: need = remap(clean canvas minus plate (public, W-3) minus (card + rest) / arch) minus veil x colour, on the haze grid. The arch factor (the sky and haze are 25 percent darker inside the wide loops mask, on purpose) is divided out so the move does not undo it. Median (share 50) along azimuth over 2 degrees, Gaussian 0.3 degrees in azimuth, 0.05 growing to 0.25 degrees in elevation, in over 0.03 to 0.1 degrees, out over 1.2 to 2.2 degrees.
- Numbers (scene luminance, median over the seen azimuths): short before -0.0009 (0.08 degrees), -0.0011 (0.14), -0.0020 (0.2), -0.0023 (0.27 and 0.36), -0.0020 (0.5), -0.0016 (0.75), -0.0018 (1.0), +0.0008 (1.5), +0.0009 (2.0), +0.0003 (2.5), -0.0001 (3.0); p10 -0.004 to -0.007 below 1 degree. Short after: within 0.0003 of 0 up to 0.75 degrees, -0.0006 at 1.0.
- A wide range (out over 3 to 4 degrees) was tried and dropped: it brings the canvas's smoky wisps at 1.5 to 4 degrees into the haze as blobs between azimuth 0 and 15 degrees (picture `out/work/sun/h1p/k2-haze-first-try-over-toward-over-difference-x6.png`). The rim is below 1 degree; the range stays 1.2 to 2.2.
- Second cause, in the shader: the fringe stretches the plate in elevation by `lift = 1 + 0.22 x (sway - rest)` (0.56 to 1.44) from the limb up. Between the limb and 0.25 degrees the plate holds "canvas minus veil x rise" and the haze holds the veil x rise, two halves of one picture; stretching one against the other gives a band of about veil x rise' x el x (lift - 1) (0.005 to 0.02 scene units) that comes and goes along the limb at every frame but 90. The old analytic glow had the same fault. Cure in `fringe.ts`: the stretch starts at `HAZE_RISE` (`haze.json` `riseDeg`): `still = min(max(el, 0), HAZE_RISE)`, sampled elevation `still + (max(el, 0) - still) / lift + min(el, 0)`. The flames still dance above 0.25 degrees; the beat (brightness) is unchanged.
- The streak add to the haze is gone from `sky.py` (the first try put the tails of the straight line into the haze, where they showed as bright patches at azimuth -25 degrees): the tails are out of the loops card and nowhere else.
- 03:42 `out/work/sun/chain5.sh` started (log `chain5.log`, ends with `finished`): `sky.py`, loops gain 0, build, frame 90 at review into `base90.jpg`, gain 1, `plate.py`, W-3 plate back from keep, `sky.py` again (must give the same `haze.png`), build, drafts 90, 0, 30, 60 into `out/work/sun/h2`.

## Item 4, chain5 looked at: the dark rim is gone, a light hairline at the limb row found and traced (03:46 to 03:52 UTC)

- `chain5.sh` ran 03:42 to 03:46. `haze.png` after the second `sky.py` run is the same bytes as after the first (the haze does not depend on `base90.jpg` or the loops card). Drafts in `out/work/sun/h2`.
- `python3 out/work/sun/limbprof.py FRAMES DIR,DIR,...`: median luminance by row from 12 rows above to 5 below the limb row, columns 330 to 460 and 1450 to 1700, frame 90 also for the approved image. Frame 90, left, rows -12 to -3: approved 33.1 33.9 34.7 35.8 37.9 38.6 38.9 39.8 40.2 41.5; before (`o3on`) 36.1 36.7 38.3 39.7 40.6 42.1 43.6 44.3 45.2 45.3 (the plateau, 3 to 5 levels over); now 33.3 34.1 35.9 37.3 37.9 38.5 39.8 40.9 41.3 41.4 (within 1.5). The dark line above the flames at 0, 30 and 60 is gone in the unwrapped strips (`v3-*`, `v5-*`, on looks as `i292-*`).
- New fault seen in the same strips: a light line one to two pixels high at the limb row at every frame (now minus before at rows -1, 0, +1: about +4 to +6 levels; against the approved image at 90: +4.7 and +3.6). Not the mip level of the haze (a lod limit by elevation changed nothing in the medians; the limit stays in the shader). Cause: the new card carries the smooth background `back` (15 display levels, 0.012 scene units) through the limb; the haze cancels it inside the flame layer with `res = w (plain - back)`, but the haze is clipped at zero where veil x rise is smaller than `back` (below about 0.06 degrees) and is not drawn at all below el 0, where the fringe still draws the plate (el above -0.006 rad). The old card was zero there (that was its `near` dimming).
- Cure: the haze is stored signed near the limb. `sky.py` adds `UNDER.amount` 0.02 below 0.15 degrees, fading out by 0.5 degrees (smoothstep), before the clip; `haze.json` carries `under` and `underDeg`; `fringe.ts` samples the haze from el -0.006 rad up at `max(el, 0)`, takes the same offset off again, adds the plate and clips the sum at zero. So right at the limb the pass draws plate minus `back` and the card adds `back` again.
- 03:51 `chain5.sh` started again with this (log `chain5b.log`, drafts into `out/work/sun/h4`).

## Item 5, first reading (05:00 UTC), nothing built

- The critics' words (mail 34): at 247 "granulation reads as glossy poured fluid, and the flow pinches into the pores instead of forming discrete cells"; at 360 "one busy, even, painterly noise everywhere, with no change in scale toward the limb. The sunspots all share the same starburst template, and the surface reads as over-sharpened illustration". Wanted by the Builder: finer cells with dark lanes toward the limb generated into the map, approved pixels as the low frequencies, the flow kept out of the pores, R1 at 360 (9) not harmed.
- How the map is built (`assets-src/sun/orbit/wrap.py`, recipe): hero pixels where the hero's view cosine is above 0.42, fading out to 0.2; the rest is 12 copies of the hero's central cap on the vertices of an icosahedron, and in the limb zone (50 to 125 degrees from the hero's axis) the fill is levelled to the hero's low-pass colour and local contrast (median 0.53), which is why the ring round the hero disc in the map is soft and low in contrast (`h1p/u2-wrap-whole.jpg`).
- The flow in `globe.ts`: `push` = gradient of the map luminance at lod + 1.5 (about 1 degree, 8 px at the hold) times `boil.reach` 0.0018, capped at 0.006 rad (2.7 px at the hold); the sample is taken at `d + push * swing`, swing = 0.5 (sin(2 pi t / 2.6 + slow) - sin(slow)), zero at holdMid. Round a dark pore the gradient points outward, so with swing above 0 the pore shrinks and the cells round it stretch toward it: the pinch. First test to make: final 247 with `boil.reach` 0 beside now, then a weight on the push that is zero in and round dark pores (`wide` luminance is already sampled further down in `main`).

At the foot base the card and the ground do not move against each other (the feet stand there), so a low-pass veil there is safe.

"MAIL 76 from the Builder, 06:02 UTC. Whole film round 30 is closed. The scroll stop critic gave the opening 7 with one major: the arch is fully formed in frame one and the later frames only shimmer inside the same silhouette; it asks for a clearly visible pulse from one footpoint over the crest to the other before the hook ends. A message that starts `DONE` with the strips section F asks for, or `PART` at 07:30 UTC. No questions needed: decide, note, continue."

Section F of `assets-src/shared/ROUND31-FIXES.md` (main 2b50ee2), verbatim: "The scroll stop critic, 7 of 10, one major, on frame 0 and eight frames across the first two seconds at 400 px wide: "The glowing solar arch reads immediately against black space and could stop a scroll, but it is already fully formed in frame one. The later frames mostly shimmer within the same silhouette, so there is little visible payoff for staying." Fix named: "Keep the bright arch readable in frame one, then send a clearly visible pulse from one footpoint over the crest to the other before the hook ends." The cure: frame 0 stays as readable as it is. Inside the first `film.hookSeconds` seconds one event changes the picture at 400 px wide: a bright surge that leaves one footpoint, climbs the arch, crosses the crest and comes down into the other foot, lighting the strands it passes and flaring the second foot as it lands, big enough that the silhouette of the light changes from frame to frame at phone size. It is plasma flowing along the loop ("the surface boils and plasma flows along the loops"), not a flash of the whole frame. The launch and landing flares left `uFoot` (which now carries only the standing pulse) and sit in the surge block (its gate is now `uSurge.y + uFlare.x + uFlare.y > 0.0`, since the flare outlives the surge amount). ## MAIL 116 (Builder, 09:16 UTC Nov 22), verbatim

"PART of 109 A received and looked at (both strips): take 2 does what was asked, frame 60 is plainly another picture at 400 px, and the eruption reads as an event. Go on to the finals as you planned. Decisions on your weak points, so you do not wait for me:
1. Less hot. The lit bundle must not be a white sheet at native size: from 38 to 60 the strands have to stay countable across the middle of the arch, as they are at frame 23. Cap it so the brightest strands clip only in their cores and the gaps between strands stay clearly darker (aim: in the arch box at native size no more than about a third of the lit pixels over 250, and the gaps at least 40 codes under the strands). Take the heat out of the wide glow and the 0.4 afterglow before you touch the front itself. The size change and the travelling front carry the hook, not the white.
2. The wide tan haze outside the arch: keep only what hugs the bundle (a few strand widths). No haze over the starfield and none that reads as fog between arch and sky at native size. If in doubt, cut it.
3. The pale band on the far ground right of the foot: keep the pool on the ground at the foot, lose the band along the limb.
4. The settle to frame 84 is accepted. Frames 90, 247, 360 and frame 0 keep their bytes at the final tier, as before; send the hashes.
5. The 15 percent line is measured on finals at 400 px, as you say; if it fails, the glow goes first, then the afterglow.
Then B (the leave on agent/sun-leave) as mailed. Times as in MAIL 109: PART of B by 09:55, DONE of both by 10:25 UTC, with the nine finals of the hook as a 3 by 3 strip at 400 px a frame and the native crops of crest and feet at 23, 45, 60."

## The leave, branch agent/sun-leave from main 8f13066 (Nov 22, 09:30 to 10:10 UTC)

The full notes of the polish are on `agent/sun` (22dc89c), those of the hook on `agent/sun-hook2` (bff4907, code b9fb48c, accepted for engine batch 21 by MAIL 123). This section is the memory of the leave only.

### Mails

- MAIL 109 B: frame 495 at 6 of 10. Majors: thin saturated red prominences with no glow, a soft airbrushed photosphere, a faint greenish grey streak detached from the corona. Cure wanted: an exposure change of ONE Sun; globe, corona and prominences rise in level together until the disc clips to the pale white of Mercury's plate, the granulation washes out, the prominences burn out pale, wide and hot, and only then the plate takes over. 450 to 470 and 540 on keep their bytes. Wanted: draft strip 474 to 502 every second frame, finals of 482, 490, 495, native crops before and after, owners of the three majors.
- MAIL 122: the plate's centre is the engine's Sun; one centre, one radius, one limb at every frame of 482 to 499. DONE or PART by 10:25 (the Builder's clock runs about 5 minutes ahead of this VM).
- MAIL 123: hook accepted as it is (b9fb48c into batch 21); the flash line is meant against main at the same frame; the three weak points go on the polish list; go on with the leave; the draft recheck of 0, 90, 247, 360 with it.
- Mail of `/root/mercury_builder` (plate on `agent/mercury-leave`): a table of the plate's disc at 1920 by 1080, mid shutter t = (f + 0.25) / 30. Limb px from the engine's Sun, left, right, up, down: 488: 345.9, 312.9, 339.1, 356.1; 495: 352.7, 325.9, 348.3, 361.4; 498: 356.6, 332.5, 353.0, 364.3. Weight of the plate = smoothstep of (u - 0.25) / 0.55, u = (f - 450) / 90: 0.239 at 488, 0.440 at 495, 0.560 at 499. The plate is additive (One, One), no alpha, fixed in Mercury's hold camera frame. At weight 1 its disc inside 0.8 radii reads 249 239 208. Its new head adds limb darkening about the stated disc.

### What the layers are at 495 (owners of the three majors)

- Red prominences: mine (`prominence.ts`, drawn at the set's weight 0.33). Cured here by the burn.
- Soft swirl photosphere: Mercury's `sunPlate` (picture `sun-plate.webp`, weight 0.44 at 495) added over my dimmed globe. My part under it is now a flat pale disc, so what texture is left at 494 to 502 is the plate's.
- The greenish grey streak: Mercury's `streak` layer (`lens-streak.webp`). It is in main's 495 and in mine at the same place (native x 860 to 960, y 300 to 312 at 1920 wide).
- Minor: the loops along the lower limb are painted in the plate; the grey white rays are my corona.
- The travel's weights: my set falls over u 0.42 to 0.55 (`SUN_SWAP` in `film.ts`), the plate rises over u 0.25 to 0.8. Where my set is gone the plate stands at 0.57 and its disc reads about 242 227 185, pale, not white.

### The finding that cost the time: the plate's stated disc is not its painted disc

- `src/stops/mercury/sunplate.json` states the disc at the picture's centre with radius 0.227585 of the picture. The painted disc in `sun-plate.webp` has its centre at 0.5176, 0.5207 and radius 0.2506 (`assets-src/sun/leave/disc.py`, 405 of 1440 rays, median residual 0.67 px of 2048; the left edge of the picture is hidden by white glow, so the fit is good to a few px, and the painted disc is not a perfect circle: on screen it stands about 10 px inside the fit at the lower left).
- On screen at 495, limb px from the engine's Sun, left, right, up, down, lower left, lower right: stated 352.7, 325.9, 348.3, 361.4, 352.0, 350.1; painted 358.9, 381.4, 413.9, 363.0, 343.4, 382.5. So the table of the plate's owner (which my script gives back to 0.1 px from the stated disc) is up to 65 px away from the limb one sees (55 on the right). Take 2 fitted my globe to the table and showed a second limb, a 28 px band at the lower left. Mercury's limb darkening is centred on the stated disc too, so it is off centre on the painted one.
- The plate is `uv = (p.xy / -p.z - rect.xy) / rect.zw` with `p` the view ray in the plate's own frame; in `sun.travel` that frame is fixed in the world (quaternion -0.19919, 0.05403, 0.79560, 0.56958, logged from Mercury's set), `shrink` is 0. So the painted limb is a fixed cone of world directions, and its ellipse on screen follows from the camera's rotation alone, with no table and no knots.

### What is built (all in `src/stops/sun/`, numbers in `look.leave`)

- `globe.ts`: `uOpen` (exposure factor) and `uWash` (flat colour, amount), inside a conditional loop before the gain line: `col = mix(col * uOpen, uWash.xyz, uWash.w)`.
- `prominence.ts`: `uBurn` (amount, wide, level, cover) and `uHot`; a wide `textureGrad` sample, then the card goes to the hot colour times its own light.
- `corona.ts`: `update` takes the edge width.
- `leave.json` (recipe in RECIPE.md, section Leave): the cone.
- `set.ts`, for `kind === 'travel'` only: `going` opens the exposure by 4 over u 0.228 to 0.4, `flat` washes the granulation out over u 0.26 to 0.4, `burnt` burns the prominences over u 0.228 to 0.36, `fitted` over u 0.228 to 0.378 moves the globe's outline onto the painted limb of the plate: an affine map in camera space (`orbit.matrix`, `matrixAutoUpdate` off) that carries the ellipse of my globe's limb onto the ellipse of the cone. The flat colour is `pale` times a level that keeps my disc plus the plate at the plate's final level while my weight falls. The corona's centre follows and its inner circle is set to the short axis.
- TRAP: draft frames render at mid shutter, t = (f + 0.25) / 30, and the final tier samples up to u 0.2278 at frame 470. Every ramp of the leave starts at u 0.228 so frame 470 keeps its bytes.

### Measured (draft tier, 1920 by 1080)

- Bytes equal to main 8f13066: 450 c69ccebcf3e4e33c, 460 2774a4074ce3ebb9, 470 4b277a95f2af3d79, 540 23691a51b96d7520, 560 c5ffb11d09ac1a2c, and the holds 0 0679ccde53820e23, 90 bf614a99e8524fbe, 247 88c6f79c3b1fff8d, 360 fcbfbf2f25bffb63.
- My limb alone (plate and corona hidden) against the painted limb, px from the engine's Sun, left, right, up, down, lower left, lower right, upper left, upper right: 488 mine 351.5, 364.3, 403.1, 357.1, 336.0, 372.8, 395.7, 373.2 against 351.8, 365.6, 402.6, 357.6, 336.3, 374.0, 395.4, 373.7; 492 mine 354.9, 372.5, 409.1, 359.9, 339.4, 377.3, 398.7, 382.3 against 355.4, 374.0, 408.6, 360.4, 339.9, 378.5, 398.5, 383.0; 495 mine 358.3, 379.6, 414.4, 362.6, 342.8, 381.1, 401.5, 390.3 against 358.9, 381.4, 413.9, 363.0, 343.4, 382.5, 401.4, 391.1; 498 (disc partly out of frame) mine 360.7, 385.9, 418.2, 363.9, 345.2, 383.9, 403.1, 397.3 against 363.0, 389.3, 419.5, 366.0, 347.4, 386.7, 404.6, 399.9. Within 1.8 px at 488 to 495, 3.4 at 498.
- Disc mean inside 200 px of the Sun's centre, every second frame 474 to 502: 204 133 65, 226 179 107, 237 207 143, 241 219 165, 242 225 176, 242 226 181, 242 227 182, 242 227 183, 242 227 184, 242 227 184, 242 227 185, 242 227 185, 241 227 184, 243 230 193, 244 232 198. It rises or holds; one step of 1 level down in red at 498. Main went 184 108 48 to 244 232 198 with the texture at standard deviation 39 falling to 20; mine falls to 6 at 484 and comes back to 16 as the plate's texture arrives.

### Weak points, open

- The fit moves and grows my disc by about 10 percent over 470 to 484 (the painted disc is that much larger than the engine's Sun at this distance). It reads as part of the opening exposure, but it is a size change.
- The painted disc is not a circle; at the lower left my limb stands about 10 px outside it.
- The plate's texture returns at 494 to 502 (Mercury's picture at weight 0.4 to 0.7): the disc is flat white at 482 to 492 and patterned again after. A white on white hand over needs the plate's own level higher earlier, which is Mercury's.
- A few thin red specks left of the burnt prominence at 490 (native x 1555 to 1620, y 520 to 555).
- `leave.json` and `look.leave.plate`, `gone`, `pale` copy Mercury's picture, rect, aim, ramp and level and the travel's swap. They must follow if those change.

### State at 10:12 UTC

- Code commit bec8e3f on `agent/sun-leave`, pushed. DONE of B sent to `/root` at 10:07 with seven pictures (`out/work/sun/lv/leave-*.jpg`), then a short mail with the final tier byte check. Looks pushed at 10:04 and 10:07.
- Finals of bec8e3f (`out/work/sun/lv/fin`): 482 e7c97c8f8bc449a8, 490 0fa41db9263749e6, 495 93cbde73bc0f2b4d. Final tier 470: 633875cb3d43af86, equal to main 8f13066.
- The red specks beside the right limb at 482 and 490 (native x 1640 to 1700, y 330 to 370 at 482) are faint debris in my prominence card, in main too but dimmer; the opened exposure lifts them. Cure when asked: a floor under the card's light before the burn (`l = max(l - floor, 0)` in `prominence.ts`), frames 450 to 470 keep their bytes as long as it sits inside the conditional loop.
- `/root/mercury_builder` has the stated against painted disc numbers. If the plate's picture, rect or aim moves, run `disc.py` and `fit.py` again and refit.
- Tools of this branch (not committed, in `out/work/sun/lv/`): `strip.py DIR LABEL`, `ell.py` (ellipse of a clean disc by threshold), `sheets.py` (the pictures of the DONE mail), `sunpx.json` (engine Sun px per frame). To see my globe alone: hide `sunPlate`, `lens`, `streak`, `glare` in Mercury's `set.ts` after `streak.update(...)` for the render only and `git checkout` the file after.

4. The open items in kit/PROGRESS.md (numbers) that this hash answers, and anything it makes worse.
5. For an engine branch: the files outside your own folders that the commit touches.

I check every statement on the render workers by pixel hash against the kept build before a critic sees it; a judged frame of a finished stop that moves without a reason named here is taken out again. The words of round 32 are in scorecards/round-032/SUMMARY.md on main and under Open items 79 to 100. Keep working after the cut on what is left for round 34, worst first. No reply to this mail is needed beyond the CUT33 message."

- Its law for CUT34 at the hand over: plate blends One, OneMinusSrcAlpha with alpha = m * (1 - exp(-10 lum)); outside 1.1 to 1.3 of its limb radius m = 1 faded out over u 0.72 to 0.99, inside m = 1 - w^2 with w my set's weight (0.21 at 490, 0.91 at 495, 1 from 500). Inside that radius its gain is raised by max(0.583 - shown(u), 0) * (1 - w^2) / shown(u), shown = smooth(ramp(0.25, 0.8, u)). Its painted white rim (0.8 to 0.94 radii) takes the hue of the disc inside in sun.travel, limb darkening 0.42 0.56 0.7.
- "Draw order, measured: your globe is in the opaque pass, so my mask covers it; your corona (renderOrder 6) and loops (5) are drawn after my plate (-10), so they lie over my disc and bloom and my mask cannot touch them. The step that is left at 495 is therefore your corona cut by its depth test at your globe's edge, on top of my bloom: row y 200, x 1064 [179 150 107] then x 1068 [180 140 85]; my limb is at x 1094 there (26 px inside your edge at that row, radius ratio about 1.07 on the left). With your globe edge on my limb the cut falls on my limb and the step is gone."

## 14:25 UTC Nov 22: the annulus at 495 found and cured, the burn filtered, the flash onset eased (for CUT34)

- Cause of the annulus (Mercury's 13:48 and 14:05 findings): the plate's aim in `sun.travel` is no longer the one `leave.json` was fitted to. Logged again from Mercury's set (one `console.error` of `sunAim`, taken out again; frames 470, 486, 490, 495 give the same value): -0.1891262280154618, 0.10468355614156852, 0.6530252190059835, 0.7258310315027776. `leave.json` held -0.19919, 0.05403, 0.79560, 0.56958 (from bec8e3f0). The two differ by 25.0 degrees about the direction of the Sun, so the painted disc kept its place and size but its long axis turned (the painted limb is a 10 percent elliptic cone because the disc sits 25 degrees off the plate's axis). My globe followed the old cone: at 495 its left edge at row 200 was x 1065, the new cone gives 1089, Mercury measured its limb at 1094.
- Cure 1, `assets-src/sun/leave/fit.py` holds the new aim and `src/stops/sun/leave.json` is written again (recipe updated). It copies Mercury's aim: if the plate's aim, rect or picture changes again, both scripts run again. A check for the next time: `out/work/sun/hk3/edge.py CONE.json FRAME PNG...` prints the edge of a plate off or plate alone draft against the cone per angle.
- Cure 2, `set.ts`: the fit is now a linear map about the camera, `mFit = [l00 l01 -cx; l10 l11 -cy; 0 0 1]` in camera axes with `c = fitted * (p - B m)` (p the centre of the plate's ellipse in the picture plane, m the centre of the sphere's own ellipse, B the map between the two ellipses). It maps the sphere's tangent cone exactly onto the plate's cone at every depth, so the `near` fudge is gone. Globe edge against the cone, drafts with the plate off: 0.4, 0.5 and 0.6 px rms at 486, 490, 495 (before: 2.7 px rms against the old cone).
- Cure 3, `corona.ts`: new uniform `uFit` (mat3, the inverse of the fit in world axes, identity outside the leave), `dir = normalize(uFit * vPos)`, and `uCenter` is the unfitted `world.position` again. The corona is drawn as that of the unfitted sphere and warped by the same map, so its b = 1 is the fitted globe's limb at every angle; no falloff band shows between the two.
- `prominence.ts`: the burn's wide sample is a bicubic B-spline of 4 bilinear `textureLod` taps on each of two neighbouring levels, blended by the fraction of the lod (the lod from the gradients times `wide` and `textureSize`). At 495 the map runs 3.5 texels to the px, so `wide 5` reads levels 4 and 5 (texels of 4.6 and 9 px on screen): bilinear magnification of those drew the 8 to 12 px stair steps Mercury saw. Now the burnt loops have smooth edges (crops `hk3/m1-495-full-x3.jpg`, `hk3/m1-486-full-x3.jpg`). A toe on the burn's luminance was tried (0.03, 0.12) and dropped: the grey film and the red squiggles left of the loops at 486 are the corona map's own painting (they are in the corona alone draft), not the burn.
- The prominence map's rgb is not premultiplied and carries garbage where alpha is low (blocks of green and blue under 8 times gain). The burn reads rgb alone. Left as it is: burnt to white the garbage does not show.
- `loops.ts`, `look.ts`: the flash's onset is eased, `flash.ease 0.017 hook` (one frame): the law is `(t/peak) exp(1 - t/peak) (1 - exp(-t²/ease²))`. Flash at frame 26 is 0.45 of its peak (was 0.67), 27 is 0.89. Change per frame at 390 px: 4.42 at 25, 7.83 at 26, 6.83 at 27, 4.60 at 28 (was 4.4, 9.17, 5.2, 4.6). Drafts of 30, 34, 38, 45, 60 keep the bytes they had before the ease.
- Leave ramp on the new fit (drafts every second frame, `hk3/l6`): change per frame at 390 px 1.63 at 452, 2.97, 4.20, 4.93, 5.27 at 460, 5.23, 5.04, 4.84, 4.68, 4.57 at 470, 4.51, 4.50, 4.51, 4.49, 4.49 at 480, 4.58, 4.69, 4.84, 5.00, 5.14 at 490, 5.23, 5.19, 4.93, 4.53, 4.18 at 500. Every frame 484 to 500: largest change of the change 0.25.
- Draft hashes that kept their bytes through all of this: 0 5342016c9cd04611, 90 bfdd36314523fa58, 247 68370f2dcd71f552, 360 316225d6ff3b7962, 450 0a5d16203d74b691.
- Measure on 4f72047d (before these cures, they touch only frames 26, 27 and 451 to 500): G13 0 of 217 frames soft, G5 pass (coverage 0.532, 0.520, 0.511, 0.313, 0.309), G17 pass (median 1.415, 1.392, 1.216, 1.019).

## MAIL 192 from the Builder, 17:36 UTC, verbatim

MAIL 192 from the Builder to sun_builder. Round 34's critics on your frames (build main 89ced51b, engine 096721f26986d35a), word for word; pack file names with the frame beside them. Use them for your CUT35 message, due 18:30 UTC, on the round 35 terms you have: take outs and small proven things, and one larger cure of a severe open item as its own commit, proved whole on review sheets beside round 34's. No reply needed to this mail. Scores: R1 8 (round 33: 6, kept build 9), R3 6 and 6, R6 9 (round 33: 5), R7 8 and 10 and 10, R4 6 (round 33: 7, kept build 6). The motion critic reads the flow of the granulation as a slide of the texture across a still limb and still spots (34 px by frame 449 in the hold, and the near ground of the opening unrecognizable by frame 81). Brandon asked for visible plasma motion on the Sun, so the motion stays; what the critic asks is that it reads as convection in place or as one turn of the whole globe, not as one layer creeping over another. If one number does that (the drift of the granulation layer down to the rate of the spots, with the boil kept), proved on the hold sheet and the terrain sheet beside round 34, it is a small proven thing. The scroll critic's 9 is the eruption of take 3; it stays. R1's two majors are the near ground of frame 90 (open item 101): your take out W-3 with its pair is wanted for round 35. What I checked myself: the grey patch on the bottom limb left of centre at 247 is in both rounds.
R1-sun, score 8:

- major, pair-03-b.png (sun.terrain 90), Near foreground across the lower third, strongest from bottom center to lower right: "The surface is more sharply outlined and locally re-patterned. Thick dark seams and polished, high-contrast cell centers replace the softer, more directionally stretched texture in the target, making the foreground read flatter and more metallic." Fix named: "Match the target's softer near-field focus and diagonal texture flow, especially at lower right. Reduce the contrast on the seams without smoothing away detail at the horizon."

- major, pair-04-b.png (sun.terrain 90), Crops D, E and F; foreground in G: "The large foreground cells have harder reddish-black borders and more isolated specular peaks. E and F lose the target's continuous diagonal stretch, while D and G lose some of its smooth falloff into foreground blur." Fix named: "Restore the directional stretch and focus gradient. Soften the seam edges and blend the bright cell centers into the surrounding texture."

- minor, pair-04-b.png (sun.terrain 90), Central sunspot in A and the adjacent rim in F: "The black opening looks slightly fuller and more uniformly black, especially at its left end, and the bright radial fibers look harder against it." Fix named: "Bring back the target's less uniform shadow and ragged edge, and ease the contrast of the brightest radial fibers."

- minor, pair-03-b.png (sun.terrain 90), Sky above the horizon, including the space inside the main arch: "Individual stars are brighter, larger and more conspicuous against the black, competing with the finer plasma strands." Fix named: "Dim and tighten the brightest star points while retaining the diffuse dust band and deep sky blacks."

- minor, pair-04-b.png (sun.terrain 90), Sky in B, C, H and I, and behind the inner strands in A: "The field has more prominent white and blue pinpoints, so the dust lane and dark space behind the arches read less softly than in the target." Fix named: "Lower stellar peaks and shrink the larger points without removing the target's faint dust structure."

- minor, pair-04-b.png (sun.terrain 90), Plasma strands in H and the horizon around both footpoints in G and I: "The filaments are a little more crisply separated from their glow, and the footpoint highlights sit more starkly against the amber horizon." Fix named: "Soften just the hottest filament edges and the transition out of both footpoints, keeping the arch and horizon geometry."

- minor, pair-01-b.png (sun.hold 360), Star field and dust band on both sides of the disk: "More of the stars stand out as bright hard pinpoints, while the right-hand orange dust knots are slightly less distinct." Fix named: "Restrain the point highlights and recover the diffuse warm structure in the right-hand dust band."

- minor, pair-02-b.png (sun.hold 360), Sky in B, C, D, E, H and I: "Larger and brighter star points are visible at crop scale. The diffuse copper structures in I look flatter next to them." Fix named: "Reduce star size and peak brightness, and restore the target's dusty warm-to-cool contrast in I."

- minor, pair-02-b.png (sun.hold 360), Disk center and bright active regions in A and F; upper disk in G: "Bright granules and filaments read slightly crisper and more individually outlined, with some loss of the target's softer transitions from white-hot cores to amber surface." Fix named: "Ease local sharpening and blend the core highlights more gradually into the gold and rust midtones."

- minor, pair-02-b.png (sun.hold 360), Upper-right, lower-left and lower-right limb loops in C, D and E: "Some of the red and gold prominence edges are more defined against the creamy corona, and the very faint strands are less integrated into its haze." Fix named: "Blend the outer prominence edges back into the soft coronal rays and retain the fine, low-contrast strands."
R3-sun-part1, score 6:

- major, image-01.png (sun.terrain 60), near foreground granulation, bottom third (crops S-W in image-04, D/G in image-02): "the granules look like glossy wet plastic or molten caramel. They have hard specular hotspots, a fine scratch-like streak texture inside each cell and a sculpted, airbrushed look. They read as a rendered material, not glowing plasma" Fix named: "remove the specular/gloss term and drive the granules with emissive brightness only (bright cell centres, dark lanes), with softer, smoother intensity inside the cells"

- major, image-01.png (sun.terrain 60), coronal loops over the sunspot (crops A, C, F, H in image-02; K, L in image-03): "the loops are made of thin, sharp, sparkler-like streaks, with spark particles and drips falling from the arcs. They look like long-exposure fireworks or a particle effect, not plasma flux tubes, and the particle origin is obvious" Fix named: "build the loops from fewer, thicker, softly glowing strands with smooth falloff along each tube; drop the falling spark particles and droplet streaks"

- major, image-05.png (sun.rise 247), bottom limb of the disc, just left of centre (about x 690-740, y 705-720; crop V in image-08): "a small desaturated grey-green smudge sits on the limb. It breaks the orange surface and looks like a texture seam or a stray patch" Fix named: "find the texture seam or bad patch at that spot on the sphere map and paint it out, or rotate the map so the seam is off the visible disc"

- major, image-05.png (sun.rise 247), corona all round the disc (crops B, D, E, G, U, V, W in image-06/07/08): "the corona streamers are opaque, milky-white brush strokes that start hard at the limb. Some of them overlap the disc edge, and they look painted rather than diffuse and emissive. The bottom streamers in V/U look like smeared paint" Fix named: "make the corona additive and much more transparent, with a radial falloff that starts behind the limb, and use fine filamentary structure instead of broad strokes"

- minor, image-05.png (sun.rise 247), disc surface (crops A, F, P, Q): "the surface texture is a busy, high-frequency mush. It is noisy and somewhat oversharpened, with no clear granulation scale, and it does not darken towards the limb" Fix named: "add limb darkening and use a cleaner granulation/supergranulation texture at a consistent scale; reduce sharpening"

- minor, image-05.png (sun.rise 247), prominences at left and right limb (crops C, D, E, H, M, O): "the prominences are saturated red ribbons with hard, cut-out edges, sitting on top of the corona like a pasted layer" Fix named: "soften the prominence edges, raise their emissive glow and blend them additively with the corona"

- minor, image-01.png (sun.terrain 60), upper limb behind the loops (crops I, N, O in image-02/03): "the spicule fringe along the horizon is a uniform comb of short hairs, repeated evenly along the limb" Fix named: "vary the length, density and brightness of the spicules along the limb"

- minor, image-01.png (sun.terrain 60), sky, upper left (crops B, J, K): "a Milky Way band is visible behind the Sun's close-up limb, and there is a dense star field right up against a blindingly bright surface. It holds up at native size, but it looks like a stock backdrop" Fix named: "dim the star field and the galactic band close to the bright limb, or remove them"
R3-sun-part2, score 6:

- major, image-12.png (sun.hold 360), crop V (912,866,456,214), southern solar globe just inside the bottom limb (also visible at bottom of disk in image-09.png): "equirectangular UV pole pinching and concentric swirling compress the solar surface texture into a desaturated pale point surrounded by stretched rings" Fix named: "use cube-sphere, triplanar, or polar-patched mapping on the solar globe so surface detail stays isotropic all the way to the poles without pinching"

- major, image-10.png (sun.hold 360), crops B, C, D, E, and G across the inner corona, spicule rim, and prominences (also in image-11.png crops K, L, M, P, R): "crisp 1-2 px white star dots shine unattenuated through the bright inner corona, 1-2 px off the limb over chromospheric spicules, and directly on top of opaque red prominence plasma (e.g., lower-right prominence base in crop E and upper-right loop in crop C), exposing an unmasked additive star layer" Fix named: "occlude the starfield behind the solar disk, spicule fringe, and prominence alpha, and extinguish stars smoothly within the bright coronal glare"

- minor, image-10.png (sun.hold 360), crops H and I along the left and right Milky Way bands (also in image-11.png crops J, O, R and image-12.png crop S): "the Milky Way background bitmap is soft and visibly upscaled at native size with smeared 4-7 px baked-in star blobs (including diagonally stretched blobs near the top-left of crop J) clashing against sharp 1-2 px procedural stars stamped on top" Fix named: "use a higher-resolution starless Milky Way dust/nebula map at native pixel scale and generate all stars with a consistent anti-aliased point-spread profile"

- minor, image-10.png (sun.hold 360), crops C, D, and E along thin outer prominence filaments and the equatorial solar limb: "fine outer prominence tendrils break into 1 px stair-stepped segments and isolated pixels at native scale, and the equatorial photospheric edge meets the spicule fringe in a slightly hard 1-2 px bright rim line" Fix named: "supersample thin prominence filaments and soften the radial opacity falloff across the photosphere-to-chromosphere limb boundary"
R4-sun, score 6:

- major, sheet-04.png (sun.hold 270 to 449), solar disk interior, frames 270 through 449: "granulation slides steadily right, about 3 pixels every 16 frames and 34 pixels by frame 449, while the limb and large features stay put" Fix named: "lock surface detail to the disk so it cannot creep across a still limb; if the globe turns, move spots, limb, and granulation together, and keep that turn nearly imperceptible"

- major, sheet-01.png (sun.terrain 0 to 179), near ground under the sunspot, frames 0 through 179: "surface texture slides down-left several pixels every 16 frames and is unrecognizable by about frame 81, while the starfield and limb barely move" Fix named: "hold granules and the sunspot registered to the locked camera, and limit change to slow convection instead of a sliding resynthesis"

- minor, sheet-02.png (sun.rise 180 to 269), frames 204 to 212, full frame: "the hold is still sharp at frame 204, and by frame 212 the pull-back is already in heavy radial blur with a wider field of view; the consecutive cover starts at 211 already at speed" Fix named: "lengthen the ease off the hold so field of view and blur ramp before the streak peaks"
R6, score 9:

- minor, image-05.png (sun.terrain 30), crest of the main solar loop: "At the peak flash, several bright strands merge into a white band at phone size, briefly obscuring the filament detail that makes the growth rewarding." Fix named: "Tame the peak highlights just enough to keep a few strands distinct while preserving the flash and the taller arc that follows."
R3-part1, score 7:

- major, image-03.png (sun.travel 495), crops M and R, right and lower edge of the Sun: "Fine surface granulation gives way to a broad, smooth yellow strip with a very clean outer edge. The contrast with the soft corona makes the limb look layered on." Fix named: "Carry finer surface structure closer to the limb and blend the brightness falloff into the base of the corona."

- minor, image-02.png (sun.travel 495), crops A and F, brightest white star in the overlapping sky area: "One bright star reads as a diffuse white bead without a tight core, unlike the nearby points." Fix named: "Tighten its core and reduce the spread of the bloom."
R4-part1, score 4:

- major, sheet-01.png (sun.travel 450 to 539), frames 458-466, solar disc: "the Sun's surface jumps from dark, high-contrast orange granulation to a washed-out pale yellow disc in 8 frames, with no matching camera move. This is an exposure or shader pop" Fix named: "ramp exposure and surface shading over at least 30 frames with an eased curve, tied to the dolly distance"
R5, score 6:

- severe, sheet-01.png (film part 1 90 to 1440), Mercury row: frames 615 and 717 upper right, 870 upper left, 1016 upper right; Venus frame 1440 right horizon versus 1185: "The sun does not shrink with distance and the light does not follow it. At Mercury it is a half-frame disk in the upper right, then a small flare in the upper left on the surface, then a half-frame disk in the upper right again. Venus is keyed from the upper left, then the cloud skim puts a still-large sun on the right horizon." Fix named: "Lock one light axis per stop and scale the solar disk to distance, not to the lens. The Mercury surface pass must see the same sun, same side, smaller than the solar close-ups and larger than Venus."

- major, sheet-05.png (film part 1 360 to 4605), hold grid, Mercury upper middle through Neptune lower right: "Stop to stop, the sun is not shown shrinking. ## NEW ROLE mail from the Builder (arrived after the cut of round 35), verbatim

NEW ROLE, from the Builder. The film is frozen and your building work is finished; nothing more of it goes onto main. For the rest of this task you are a render worker for the master of the film (every frame at the final tier). Do this now, in this order:
1. Save your own work: commit whatever is open on your own branch (no push needed). Do not edit, commit or push anything after that.
2. In your repo: `git fetch --depth 1 origin 567b3a555461d1e7d88300dbe5febc52073da828`, then `git checkout --detach 567b3a555461d1e7d88300dbe5febc52073da828`. `git rev-parse HEAD` must print that hash. Then `npm ci` and `npm run build`.
3. Read `assets-src/shared/RENDER-WORKER.md`. It is your brief from now on (its clone step is already done).
4. A render command refuses to start under 3 GB of free disk. If `df -h .` shows less than 6 GB free, delete old harness output under `out/work/` (chunks, stills, sheets, packs, clips); none of it is needed any more.
5. Proof that your VM draws the same pixels as mine: `npm run -s still -- --frame 2325 --tier draft --out /tmp/ref2325.png`, then `sha256sum /tmp/ref2325.png`. It must print fd128b1a999f76fa079027e50ace59ac82ec5083c1c53b65667f23176a9a98cf.
6. Answer with one line and nothing else: `WORKER READY <nproc> cores, <free GB> GB free, <the first 16 characters of your hash>`. If your hash differs, answer `WORKER DIFFERENT <your full hash>` instead and stop.
Then wait. Your jobs come in the next message.

- State when it arrived: branch `agent/sun` at 97bc90fc (pushed), READY of CUT35 02e8dbc1, working tree clean. No word had come on whether the number (20d06b43) was taken.

# TASK OF MAIL 212 (23 November 2026): the place on the Sun, branch `agent/sun-place`

This is the task now. It replaces everything above as the thing to do; the sections above are history and knowledge. The mail in full:

> MAIL 212 from the Builder (/root). A new task for you, sun_builder. It replaces anything you still held. Answer with one line as soon as your new branch exists: "ACK 212 <branch> <commit>".
> 
> What happened since your last work. The film was frozen (main, commit 567b3a55), the record round ran, the master, the nine clips and the nine posts were made. Brandon watched it and wrote this note at 05:57 UTC on 2026-11-23. It outranks the kit's cover rules:
> 
> "The main issue I have is the transitions between the planet view and the moment before the surface view comes in, it's a blurry mess, and it's obviously a transition. I think the goal would be to make it seamless. Like there's actually a place on the planet that the camera's going to. Hey next, the surface for Jupiter, and Saturn both look completely weird. It looks like an image that is being stretched and contorted it shouldn't look like that. It should look like it's actually generated out of code."
> 
> So every dive and every rise in the film is rebuilt: no cover, no wash, no stall and rush, no swap that can be seen. The spec is cut/HANDOVER.md on main. Part B of it is yours for sun.
> 
> Setup
> - git fetch origin main, then git checkout -b agent/sun-place FETCH_HEAD. Main holds the kept build. Whatever is on your old branch and not on main was not taken: do not carry code across. Carry your notebook across if yours is newer than main's (assets-src/sun/NOTES.md), and commit it first.
> - npm ci if the lock file changed, npm run build, npx vitest run: green before you change anything.
> - Read whole, from disk: cut/HANDOVER.md; kit/PROGRESS.md section "Review note of 2026-11-23" and the open items 149, 111, 131 and 46 to 53 (the critics' words on your dive and rise in the frozen build); AGENTS.md and kit/addenda/ again; your own notebook.
> - Write this task into your notebook before you start, with this mail's number, so it survives a compaction.
> 
> The task, in this order
> 1. S1 now: solve the camera of your approved close image (references/approved/sun-close.jpg) against the sphere at your spot and write src/stops/sun/place.json. Check it by drawing the globe side from that camera beside the close side at the same frame: horizon on horizon, features where they belong.
> 2. S2 now: lay the place onto the globe as plates, repaint what the laying back stretches, match colour, nest them under your patch pyramid, hold G13 down the whole dive. Proof: the 12 frame top down dive sheet with no blur.
> 3. S3: one steady move for dive and rise by the rules of E3. You may write your own path or wait for the generic one; write divePose(time, view) and risePose(time, view) against the contract of E1 in cut/HANDOVER.md either way.
> 4. S4 and S5 when I tell you that E1 and E2 are on main (the engine is being built now by another agent, handover_engineer, and proven on Mars first): merge main, opt in, remove your cover, bring light, exposure, haze, Sun and sky to one function of time through T, and run npm run handover for dive and rise.
> 
> What may not change: your hold segment and your terrain segment. Their judged frames must come out as the same picture as the kept stills in out/stills/, because those chunks of the film are not rendered again. The travel after your rise is not rendered again either: your rise must end in the pose, the speed and the look that travel starts with today. The timeline does not change.
> 
> Your stop
> - Frames: terrain 0 to 179, rise 180 to 269, hold 270 to 449. Judged: 0, 60, 90 (terrain), 247 (rise), 360 (hold). The Sun has no dive: the film opens in the close view. Your work is the rise, 180 to 269, and it must end in the hold's first pose and speed as today.
> - The place is the sunspot with its loops, over the curved limb of the close image. As the camera pulls back, that sunspot with its arch has to stay in the picture as a place on the disc, shrinking, until it is one spot on the whole Sun. The granulation is alive on both sides: at T both sides must show the same cells in the same state, which is what the place picture of E2 gives; your plates and your globe side functions must agree with it around it.
> - What the critics wrote about the rise of the frozen build: "the cover is an unaligned cross-dissolve that double-exposes two mismatched rigs: a steeper globe limb with bright white coronal streamers, upper-right red prominences, coarse mottled orange-white surface blotches, and a bright angled Milky Way band fades in directly over the flatter chromosphere horizon, central sunspot umbra, glowing magnetic arch, and dark starfield of the terrain view"; "the camera lacks mass and smooth easing in rise: it keeps creeping closer to the sunspot through frames 180-196, abruptly reverses into a violent whip-zoom that covers nearly the entire surface-to-full-disk pullback in just 33 frames (196-229), and then sits nearly parked for frames 245-269"; "thin 1px yellow filament loops near disk center (frames 216-222) and stars in the upper-right corner remain razor-sharp on top of a heavily radially smeared photosphere".
> - The old cover was a flare bloom. It goes. Corona, streamers, prominences and the sky must be one function of time through T: nothing may fade in over the close view.
> 
> Working rules
> - Your folders only: src/stops/sun/, assets-src/sun/, public/assets/sun/. Push only to agent/sun-place. Never main, never force, never rebase. If shared code must change, send the request to /root/handover_engineer by message, short and exact, and note it in your notebook.
> - Looks at least every 30 minutes of working time, also while something renders, good or bad: npm run looks -- --push --folder sun-place --note "one line".
> - Draft tier to iterate, review tier to prove. One render process at a time. Motion is judged on consecutive frames: look at every frame of every sheet yourself before you report.
> - Image generation only through the skill named media, at most 6 takes per asset per round, recipe in assets-src/sun/RECIPE.md, binaries committed once per milestone. No file over 95 MB. Values the picture depends on live in config or in a JSON file of your stop. No Math.random, Date.now, performance.now on a pixel path.
> - Every commit message ends with the trailer: Co-authored-by: Perplexity <noreply@perplexity.ai>. No em dashes, no en dashes, no exclamation points, no model names in any file or message.
> - Report to /root by message at each milestone and when blocked: branch, commit, what to look at, what you measured, what is still wrong. Do not wait for an answer unless blocked. M1 is S1 with its check picture and the first laid back plate, rough is fine, within about 90 minutes. M2 is S2 and S3 with their proofs. M3 is the handover, dive and rise, with the E4 reports and the dense sheets.
> - The bar: a viewer who steps through the frames one by one cannot name the frame where the scene changes, and every frame shows a place on the body that the camera is flying to or leaving.

  - Fault 1, colour speckle on the map's white plage from frame 228 (purple, green, blue in the cell lanes). Cause: the map colour there is close to white, so its inverse tone curve gives very large linear values with channel noise, and the dark lanes of the cell detail pull them down into the range where the noise shows as hue. First try (scalar cell detail instead of a ratio per channel) did not cure it. Cure: the map's colour under the cells is held to red >= green >= blue, and the tone handover is a geometric mix (close ^ share * map ^ (1 - share)), so the plage comes in evenly with height and not at once.
  - Fault 2, a spike standing off the left loop foot from above (frames 216 to 240). Cause: the legs of the loops that lie over ground in the close image are held to the ground by the sheet (`t <= 1.15 x ground distance`, `pin`), so from above they run along the ground toward the limb. The sheet may not change (the terrain segment is kept). Cure: from above (`heights.strands`) the sheet is trimmed by true distance from the middle of the feet (`look.loops.away`, in half foot distances), beside the trim by arc angle (`look.loops.trim`).

No second lookup of the plate any more (flow amount 0): nothing is dragged, the life is light. Only cells whose whole edge is lane may close and open.

### 10:35 to 10:50 UTC, the rebuild for mail 241, what was decided

  - `flow`: two layers of lanes across the strands, a coarse one (bundles about 28 px high at the top) and a fine one (about 10 px). Lanes are equal steps of the height of a strand's top, so they are about equally wide in the frame (the old lanes were steps of the angle and grew wide on the outer loops). In each lane clumps with a spacing, a length and a speed of the lane's own run from one foot over the top to the other; a clump is a soft head with a tail. Speeds 0.4 to 1.2 of the distance between the feet per second (about 10 to 29 px a frame at the pose of frame 90), a share of the lanes (`back`) runs the other way. Every lane set is seen through a window and a second set half a lane apart fills the gaps, so no seam between lanes can show.
  - First take (10:54 UTC, dropped): three layers that ADDED light in the clumps. The heads moved at 20 to 27 px a frame but a head added only about a quarter in display levels: the strands sit near the top of the tone curve, there is no room above them. So the contrast has to come from below.
  - Second take (11:01 UTC, kept): each layer is a FACTOR on the strand's light, `low` between the clumps and `low + amount` in a clump, and `amount` is computed from spacing, length and share so that the mean over time is one: the loops keep their brightness on average. The two factors multiply. What a clump adds above one is bent over by `knee` where the strand is bright already, so a clump cannot burn the strand out.
  - Tuning seen at native size, camera frozen: lanes 30 and 115 with lengths 0.06 and 0.03 and lows 0.45 and 0.5 moved plainly but broke the strands into dashes (it read as sparks, not as the loops of the image). Lows 0.6 and 0.7 were too faint, 0.45 and 0.6 visible, 0.38 and 0.58 is the present value.

Measure (mean of the 2 by 2 checker filter in the box x 800 to 1000, y 700 to 780, frozen): frame 60 20.1, 120 21.6 against 9.4 at frame 90; with `inflow.amount` 0: 9.4 and 9.8. - Why the first tries were weak (takes g1 to g4, 12:00 to 12:25):
  - The tone curve is flat on the bright ground, about 23 display levels per stop. Life has to be set in stops (`lin *= exp2(S)`), a tenth of the light does nothing.
  - The cell labels of `cells.py` are rough: the flood cuts bright cells along straight lines and one label often spans several cells of the picture. Rings counted from a label's middle crossed the cells of the picture as arcs (`out/work/sun/life/check-crop.jpg`, `g3-six-a.jpg`).
  - Laying a soft copy of the plate under the life (remake 0.5) washes the picture out (`g4-near.jpg`).
  - Long streaks that slide along themselves change little; what moves has to be short along its way.
- The ground as rebuilt (from take g5 on): no label in it.
  - `cells.py`: red of `cells-<name>.png` is now how deep a pixel lies in the bright ground, its distance from the nearest lane measured on the ground (rows of the picture drawn apart by how much the view flattens a cell, at most 6 times), against the size of a cell, the middle of the median cell at 1 / FULL (FULL 1.6). Plate 0.333 cell, apron 0.325 cell. The edge of the spot is no lane (a front ran along it as one long line). Green unused, blue the rate of the cell as before; the second picture as before.
  - A line showed in the motion where plate and apron meet (y 870 to 930 at pose 90): each picture finds its own lanes, so the depth differed (`out/work/sun/life/g5-d5.jpg`). Cure: the apron's depth is the plate's wherever the plate is drawn, and gives way to the apron's own over 0.01 to 0.13 of the image height in front of the line where the plate ends (`LEAD`). Seen continuous with the depth drawn as grey (`out/work/sun/life/dbg-deep.jpg`). The plate has to be made before the apron.
  - `life.ts` `alive()`: takes the place on the ground in cells (`arc / uCellSize`, same on the globe). Fronts: `u = time * rate * (1 + spread * (lot - 0.5)) + late * place + (deep + warp * made) * rings`; a whole number passes when a front does; `lot` and `late` are slow noise of the place, so neighbours are out of step and each front has its own strength. What streams: value noise of the place whose third axis is `deep * slide + time * rate`, at two sizes, only its upper half (knots of light, the mean taken off), each size fading where it would be under `px` pixels. All of it is summed in stops and masked by `seen * (1 - spot) * smoothstep(0, edge, inner)`. The closing of some cells and the slow dome stay on the labels (masked by `known`). Gone: the split, `uLifeReach`, `cells.json` in the shader.

- The flow map (`life.ts` `alive()`, values in `life.json` `flow`):
  - The picture is taken twice, each look carried along the stream by `travel` cell widths over its life (`period` seconds), the two half a life apart, mixed by a tent. What has just come up is brighter and what nears the lane darker (`age`, in stops).
  - Carrying the whole picture that far tears the frames between the judged ones (take h2: 12.61 raw, cells doubled at 93 and 95). So the picture is taken apart: its broad shapes (the plate through a footprint `blur` times as wide) run only `slow` of the way, the fine texture on them (the plate against that) runs the whole way. A cell keeps its outline and its lanes; what streams is the grain inside it.
  - Two looks half a travel apart do not agree in the fine texture and their mean is flatter than either: the pulse of sharpness with a half period of 7.5 frames seen in h5. Cure: the mean is brought back toward its strength (`keep`), and every place has its own shift of the clock (`offset`, slow noise of the place), so the ground does not beat as one.
  - Near the times in `still` (frames 0, 60, 90 as seconds from frame 90) the shifts close to nil over `ease` seconds, so there every look stands on its own place and the frame is the plate itself.

A dark point opens and runs to both ends as a lane, the stream on both sides turns toward it, the flanks shade. A gaussian dark line read as a hairline; a plateau profile with shaded flanks reads as a lane. The lane is as wide in the frame whichever way it runs (the view flattens the ground, so across is measured as the picture shows it). `cells.py ... check` lists the cells that are largest in the picture with the lane across each, which is where the sites come from. `flat` is a reserved word in GLSL.

### 15:18 to 16:xx UTC: READY sent, the two sides at T cured

- 15:18 READY sun c2524de5 sent to /root with the eight points still wrong. MAIL 291 (15:22): the check runs on twelve machines; go on with point 1; say what frame 60's mean difference to the approved image was on the frozen build. Engineer (15:19): the red test of main is fixed in 60c083e; a new option `edgeIn` (not used here, the Sun has no place picture). MAIL 293 (15:36): main is 0eae595, tests 170 of 170; merge it before the next named commit.
- Point 1, the side difference at T (frame 216, draft, `out/work/sun/life/sides.sh NAME`, the same measure as the handover tool: largest channel difference per pixel, mean and 99th percentile). Trials in this order, each measured:
  - c2524de5: mean 9.354, p99 71 (handover report). Plate and apron about 2 px apart, field 0.2 px.
  - Globe side: the spin with the sine and cosine of the CPU and the direction made unit length: plate 16.17 to 2.02 per channel, apron 14.99 to 1.96. So the suspect of 15:10 held: the turn in the shader left the direction short. What stayed was a grid in the difference picture, dark on the edges of the globe's faces and bright in their middle: the face of the mesh lies inside the ball.
  - Globe side also on the ball itself (line of sight met with the ball): mean 0.944, p99 6, shifts under 0.04 px.
  - Ground side also on the ball: mean 0.813, p99 5, shifts under 0.02 px by blocks of 180 px.
  - The same arithmetic on both sides (the globe's vertex in stage units, the ground's formula): 0.812. No gain, so the arithmetic was not the rest.
  - What the rest was, measured: the difference grows with the gradient of the picture (0.07 levels where the gradient is under 2 levels per px, 1.0 where it is 40 to 80), which is a shift, not a tone: 0.02 px mean, 0.033 px standard deviation. A map of the shift (least squares in windows of 15 px) shows rings and spokes round the foot of the eye, the rings and sectors of the ground mesh. The varyings of a mesh are interpolated by the rasteriser less finely than a tenth of a pixel, and the Sun's texture has a mean gradient of 20 levels per px, so 0.02 px is 0.4 levels. The other stops pass with 0.05 to 0.10 because their pictures are softer.
  - Second cause, seen as soft orange patches top left and right: the low haze of the ground (life.json `haze`), which the globe does not draw. 0.5 levels darker on the globe side in the top 240 rows.
- The cure as built (`/home/user/workspace/pixel-sight.diff` before the commit):
  - The place comes from the pixel itself on both sides: the line of sight of the pixel from `gl_FragCoord`, the lens (`projectionMatrix`, so the jitter of the samples is in it) and the turn of the camera into the axes of the stage (`uSightTurn`, made in set.ts by the same chain of turns as the eye), met with the ball of the stage from the eye (`uCam`, on the globe `uEye`). One uniform object for both sides (`PLACE_SHARED`), one formula. No varying of either mesh is in it.
  - Ground: only in the rise (`uExact`), so the surface view 0 to 179 keeps its arithmetic. Globe: only while the place is on (`uPlaceOn`), so the hold keeps its arithmetic; the far map still uses the old direction.

## 21:00 UTC. Mail 327 items 1 to 3: the arcade keeps moving after the release, the shell travels on, knots race down the legs

- Why: mail 327. 1 (severe) frames 58 to 100 are a quiet third. 2 (major) the release must read as a flare through motion: matter leaving the crown and travelling outward for a second or more, thrown light on the ground that rises and falls with it, knots racing down both legs after; peak under the kept clip's 149, nothing burnt to one mass. 3 (major) the pool under the left foot, frames 4 to 30 (cured in a24fe051, numbers in the entry of 18:30). 4 (major) the ground plasma repeats: measured here, not cured in this commit; mail 344 names the cure and it is the next commit.
- Built, all in `src/stops/sun/` (`loops.ts`, `ground.ts`, `set.ts`, `life.ts`, `look.ts`) with the values in `arcade.json`:
  - `ring`: a wave that runs from the crown down both legs, three hits (`hits` 0.62 s the flash, 1.52 s the launch of the knots, 2.42 s their landing), `period` 0.9 s, `fade` 1.9 s, eased out between 3.5 and 4.1 s (frames 105 to 123). The pose of the strands is pinned exactly at the calm of frame 90 while the wave passes (`uRing`, `uRingRest`, `ring()`), so frame 90 keeps the approved pose and the frames round it still move. ## 21:25 UTC. Mail 344, the ground must not come back to itself: cause, design, simulation (no render yet, the proof above holds the render slot)

- Why: mail 344. No look may return inside the 180 frames (likeness at any lag from 4 to 60 frames under 0.2 in the three ground boxes and under the spot), the lanes themselves must move (an outline is not the same after two seconds), and the two looks must not stand at equal weight long enough to show as a doubled texture. Frames 0, 60 and 90 stay the plate.
- Cause, shown in a simulation of the stream of `life.ts` on the plate at a quarter of its size (`out/work/sun/life/sim.py`; copies of the plate and of the two maps as inputs): every life of a look starts from the same picture, so the ground is the same again after one period (15 frames) and nearly the same after half a period, when the other look shows the same travel. Simulated likeness of the build as it is: 0.88 at lags 7 and 8, 1.00 at lag 15 (rendered, camera held: 0.57 and 0.53 at 7 and 8, near box).
- Tried and dropped: a jump of the look along its stream at each new life is only a shift in time (with one fixed sequence of jumps a peak of 0.56 to 0.71 at lag 50); jumps in proportion to the stream give marbled streaks.
- Chosen, as a patch (`out/work/sun/life/v15.py`, on `life.ts` and `life.json`; not applied to the tree while a measure runs from it):
  - Each life of a look takes its fine texture from another piece of the picture: a shift of the lookup by up to one travel, any way, from smooth noise of the place and of the number of the life, changed while the look counts for nothing. It is nil in the lanes and in the middle of a cell, and where the piece would come from a lane it brings no fine texture, so no lane is brought along. The look that stands at frames 0, 60 and 90 keeps its own place, so those frames keep their bytes. Simulated likeness at any lag from 4 to 60: near 0.04, mid 0.03, far 0.10 (at lag 4), under the spot 0.04. Sharpness of the near box (mean absolute Laplacian): the plate 8.93, the build as it is 8.28, with the cure 7.80 to 7.92 (4 to 6 percent softer; with a wider clearing of the lanes 7.26).
  - The change from one look to the other is steeper (`flow.fade` 0.2; tried 0.12 and 0.25 in the simulation): both weights lie between 0.3 and 0.7 for about 1.2 frames at a time instead of 3. With the shift above the two looks are no longer the same piece half a travel apart, so where both count the picture is a blend of two textures, not one texture twice.
  - The lanes move: away from frames 0, 60 and 90 the cells push harder on each other (`swell.more`) and the whole ground wanders slowly, each place its own way (`drift`: up to 0.22 of a cell, a new way about every two seconds). Simulated on the frames of the cure (`wander.py`): the share of lane area that two frames two seconds apart have in common falls from 0.86 (fixed lanes) to 0.39 at `drift.amount` 0.3 (0.71 at 0.08, 0.26 at 0.6).
- What the simulation shows about the number of mail 344: a lane that moves slowly is still near its place four frames later, so with moving lanes the likeness at lags 4 to about 12 is high by its nature and then falls for good (simulated at 0.3: near 0.58 at lag 4, 0.29 at 11, 0.11 at 15, under 0 from 22 on; at 0.08: 0.18 at most). The kept build with the camera held measures 0.92 at lag 4 for the same reason (`like.py 110 170 kepth`: near 0.92 at 4, 0.53 at 11, first under 0.2 at lag 16). Under 0.2 from lag 4 on holds with fixed lanes only. Both curves go to /root with the choice.

### G 1 design (src/stops/sun/convect.ts, numbers in convect.json)

- The field is a function of a ground point (the stage's chart: arc lengths from the point under the stage's camera) and of the life clock (seconds from frame 90). Nothing is read from a picture and nothing is kept between frames.
- Cells: the cells of a weighted diagram of seeds. Every lattice square holds two seeds whose lives run half a life apart (the life's length differs from square to square). A seed's weight rises from nil, stays, and falls to nil; at nil weight its cell is empty, so a life begins and ends without a jump and the next life starts at another place. Six of ten seeds part into two halves; the lane between the halves is there before they part. The lanes are bent by a slow warp of the chart in two sizes.
- Height: a dome over each cell (zero at the lanes, mean height taken off so the ground lies on the stage's ball on average), broad folds that run outward, a dip in the middle of old cells; the sunspot is a funnel with a pit for the umbra and ridges for the fibres. A mesh that lies still on the ground (rings close together near the point under the camera, 700 rings by 1536 sectors, 63 degrees of the ball) is displaced by the height in the vertex shader; the fragment works the same functions out again at its own point and takes the normal from their slopes.
- Colour: a brightness (lanes dark, young cells bright, old ones darker, folds and threads that travel outward from the cell's middle, fibres with grains that run toward the umbra) goes through a ramp of 16 colours measured from the ground of the approved close image by brightness class (`assets-src/sun/convect/ramp.py`), then through the grade and the inverse of the film curve as the picture ground did. A face turned to the eye is brighter than one turned away (the law of the limb, on the relief's own normal). The lights of the flare on the ground are the same uniforms as before.
- For G13 the function reports one texel a pixel (it has no texel grid).

- Jets and glow (`jets.ts`): 48000 blades rooted in a ring of the ground from 4.6 to 9.5 units from the point under the stage's camera; each throws (up, slow, back) with a period of 2.5 to 5 seconds, a function of time alone. A blade shows by the angle at which its foot is seen (grazing and beyond the limb), so none stands over ground that faces the eye. The glow of the thin layer is worked out for each ray from how near it passes the ball; it dies by angle before the ray turns away from the ball (the first version had a step there, 12 levels in the sky). The old fringe (a picture pass) is hidden while the function ground shows.

Changed: lumps on each cell (round tops with creases, two sizes, carried outward from the cell's middle in two takes half a turn apart so no take is seen to begin again), the dome steeper at its foot (shoulder 0.022) so cells press on each other, lanes with a narrow floor of their own (`gap`), brightness that follows the face turned to the eye more (limb 0.85, glint 0.3) and a darker body (top 0.27). (1) The bulb: the height is a function of the share of the way from the cell's middle to its lane, r / (r + e), so every cell is one whole round bulb whatever its size and shape, steep where it meets the lane, and a wide cell stands taller than a small one. (2) The normal is taken from the height itself (two more evaluations a pixel, a step the size of the pixel's foot), so the light follows the shape exactly, also through the warp. (3) Heat by height: the top of a bulb is the brightest and its side darker on every side (level tops bright, steep sides dark), with only a little left of the law by the eye. (4) Threads in locks, seen by the dark between them, deep on the sides and faint on the top. (5) Cells of many sizes (a share of its own for each seed in the diagram), lattice 0.2. (6) A sheen between the eye and a far light.

### 23 Nov, 03:38 to 04:15: NOTE 501 (nothing comes in by the pixel), NOTE 504, MAIL 505, MAIL 508

- NOTE 501, NOTE 504, MAIL 505 and MAIL 508 are on disk beside the others. 501: every density a fade reads is counted in 1080ths of the frame height, not in pixels of the format: one factor, the height of the drawing buffer over 1080, in what a fade reads and in nothing that only picks a mip. 504: in the pass that draws a place picture the factor is not `view.format.height` over 1080 (that is 2 at 16 by 9 there), and the uhd picture has the master's pixels, so there the factor is 1 at both. The shared helper `src/core/frame.ts` is not on main yet: my own uniform until the Builder says it is, then its source is swapped.
- `src/stops/sun/format.ts`: `formatUniform` and `setFormat(height, picture)`, value = height / (1080 * picture). `set.ts` writes it before each draw: `setFormat(view.format.height, view.place ? config.raw.handover.place.scale : 1)`.
- Where the factor went in. `globe.ts`: `parent = texels * uFormat`, `parent = gd * uFormat * uPatchScale * uPatchSize` (the place and patch fades, `uPlaceFade`, `uPatchFade`), `finer` reads `texF * uFormat`. `ground.ts`: `finer` reads `texF * uFormat`. `life.ts`: the foot of `lifeSeen` is `/ uGrad * uLifeFade.z` (it divided by z before: the wrong way round; `px` and `unit` already divide by z). `loops.ts`: the three `smoothstep(0.35, 0.8, fwidth(..) * uFormat)`. `convect.ts`: `footM = sqrt(footX * footY) * uFormat` (the cells' fade `fine`, the mottle's waves, the threads' fade read it). `set.ts`: the far Sun is drawn from a radius of 0.6 px counted in 1080ths (`radiusPx / formatUniform.value`).
- Left as they are, they only pick a mip or a step of a lookup: the gradients times `uGrad` in corona.ts, skycard.ts, prominence.ts, loops.ts, ground.ts, globe.ts; the fringe's lod by the height; `foot` in convect.ts for the steps `je` and `eps`.
- Proof (a), the factor alone, 16 by 9, draft: frames 20, 60, 200, 215, 222 against c80 and 270, 300, 449 against hold80: the same sha256, all eight.
- Proof (b), the factor alone: `npm run smooth -- --stop sun --only parity`: "smooth sun: red, 1 over the limit or not measured." "parity: mean difference between uhd scaled down and 16x9 12.810 at frame 225 (sun.rise), limit 12". Coarse mean 1.587, block 1.889, level 0.676: inside. The panel and the pair at two to one: the same cells in the same places at both formats; the difference is grain of the draft's one sample a pixel at 16 by 9, strongest toward the limb (blocks of the top corners 32 to 36, middle 2 to 8), where a cell is several pixels wide and under a pixel deep, and in lanes narrower than a pixel.
- The term, two band limits in `convect.ts` (both change 16 by 9 frames of the terrain and the rise where the ground is far; off, `light.far.flat` [1000, 1001] and `light.far.lane` 0, frames 60 and 222 keep their bytes): (1) `footL`, the long side of what a pixel covers, in the cells' measure: `fine` also falls between `light.far.flat` [0.15, 0.5] cells a pixel on that side (`uCvFlat.xy`); (2) a lane narrower than `light.far.lane` 0.5 times a pixel is drawn that wide and weaker by the same share (`laneE = max(laneW, uCvFlat.z * footC)`, `body = 1 - laneW / laneE * exp(-e / laneE)`), so it keeps the light it takes.

- Finding: near cells hide the lane and the foot of the cell behind, so a dark that is tied to the ground distance from the lane is hidden in the low view. The grade by the cap's own height is what is seen: it makes the bulb and the soft crease.
- Finding: the two halves of a cell that has not parted are two sites at one place. They were tracked as two lanes, so the second nearest lane was the nearest one along every lane and the pit term blackened all lanes (5.6 percent of the near ground under 0.05). Cure in `cvDiagram`: the second of such a pair is skipped. `e` and `ge` are unchanged by it.
- Threads (point 4a), `light.line`: a thread is a level line of one noise of the place where it leaves the lane. That place is the foot on the nearest lane by the softer way up (`cvFan`, `soft` 10), so the threads leave a lane square to it and close in between two lanes by themselves. `scale` 14 noise cells to a lattice unit, `levels` 4, `curl` 2, `wide` 1.0 (1080ths of the frame height to each side, times uFormat), `glow` [0.6, 5], `close` [0.1, 0.25] (no threads where they would stand closer than four to ten of their width: a grating of threads a pixel apart crawled), `from` [0.5, 0.75] of the way from the middle to the lane (the cap is free), `lane` 0.3, `amount` 0.3, `bunch` [4, 0.3, 0.6] (the cut to unequal lengths), `flow` [3, 1.2] (the cut travels from cap to lane: point 7).

- The distance of a pixel from a thread is read from the noise's own slope (two more lookups) times the pixel's step in the lookup place. With the noise's difference between neighbour pixels the lines were dots.
